The cumulative P&L over repeated identical bets on winners was summed without deduplication, inflating both n and P&L by ~17.5×. First-occurrence-only P&L shows the true signal: −$0.18. **Calibration by model_p bucket (n=73):** [0.00,0.25): actual 15.4% vs model 21.8% (−6pp, overconfident); [0.25,0.50): actual 13.6% vs model 35.0% (−21pp, severely overconfident); [0.50,0.75): actual 61.3% vs model 66.4% (−5pp, reasonable); [0.75,1.00): actual 42.9% vs model 79.8% (−37pp, n=7, severely overconfident). **MIP bucket performance (n=73):** MIP [0.00,0.30): underperforms market implied probability. MIP [0.30,0.50): underperforms MIP (model fires but actual win rate < MIP). MIP [0.50,0.70): +10.6pp above MIP (n=29, ~1 standard error, not statistically significant). **One positive signal:** Edge monotonicity by model_p quartile is CORRECT (Q1=11.1%, Q2=22.2%, Q3=55.6%, Q4=61.1%) — model ranks bets directionally right but calibration is severely overconfident at extremes. **Verdict: INCONCLUSIVE.** n=73 does NOT satisfy ADR-0024 Tier 2 (n ≥ 500). GG-046 remains OPEN. **Bug fix (T-208):** `already_fired_game` moved out of `_run_cycle()` to game-day scope in `main()`, reset at UTC midnight date rollover. `already_fired_game: Optional[set] = None` parameter added to `_run_cycle()` signature. 2113/2113 tests. **T4 portfolio on VPS contains contaminated data from the re-betting bug.** Next step: reset T4 portfolio and accumulate clean data. **GG-050 registered** (same-session) for the re-betting bug itself. | EL |
| 2026-06-29 | **[SESSION 100 / ADR-0034] Layer 3 Semantic Contracts: `c7_semantic_contracts.py` — GG-051 resolution** | **Governance gap (GG-051):** The project entered SESSION 100 with two governance layers: Layer 1 (architecture integrity, gate reviews) and Layer 2 (C7 Brier/ECE/P&L drift monitoring). Two independent silent failures — GG-049 (T1 MLB dark 2 game days) and GG-050 (T4 MLB re-betting 17.5×) — shared a common root cause: no governance layer asked "Is what is currently running doing what it was designed to do?" Both failures were invisible to L1 (architecture was correct) and L2 (no resolved forecasts → no Brier drift; individual positions appeared valid → re-betting invisible to ECE). **Decision (ADR-0034 ACCEPTED):** Adopt a Layer 3 governance tier: Semantic Behavioral Contracts. Layer 3 formally states the behavioral intent of each pipeline stage and each execution band and verifies it at runtime. Module: `src/prediction_market_bot/c7_monitoring/semantic_contracts.py`. **Contract taxonomy:** (1) `PIPELINE_FLOOR` — `SemanticFloorGuard.assert_floor()`: if games_fetched > 0 and matched_players == 0, raises `SemanticFloorViolation`, pipeline halts. Catches GG-049 pattern within seconds of C1 data load. (2) `EVALUATION_GATE` — `ContaminationGuard.is_clean()`: verifies data window coherence before `evaluate_forecast()` fires. Checks: has_live_quotes, mip ∈ (0,1), player_in_live_boxscore, game_in_progress, elapsed_min < regulation, h_needed ≤ ab_remaining. Returns False = evaluation skipped. O(1). (3) `BAND_INVARIANT` — `BandInvariantChecker`: per-band structural property checks. T1 MLB: ba_l10 ∈ [0.05, 0.60], threshold ∈ [1,3]. T4-PROJ: lambda_prior ≠ fallback 20.0. T4-FLOW: score_diff ∈ [-60, 60]. Returns `ContractViolation` → WARNING to C6. (4) `CORR_DOWNWARD` — ADR-0029 Principle 6: T4-CORR overlay cannot increase a position. Raises `T4CorrDownwardViolation` → caller triggers kill switch. **C7 integration:** `SemanticContractMonitor` — thread-safe, session-level violation counters, exposes `get_health_state()` for `get_health_snapshot()` / C8 dashboard. **Throughput guarantee:** All checks O(1) per evaluation; < 1ms overhead per cycle at ~1,000 evals/game. **Integration patches:** `09_INTEGRATION_PATCHES/SEMANTIC_CONTRACTS_INTEGRATION.md` — 5 patches (PIPE-05, T4 MLB `_run_cycle()`, C7 monitor, violation recording, `__init__.py` exports) with test coverage requirements and deployment sequence. **ADR-0034 file:** `03_DECISIONS/ADR-0034_layer3-semantic-contracts.md`. | DS |
| 2026-06-29 | **[SESSION 101] Principal vision, alpha taxonomy, and market efficiency gradient formalized as canonical CLAUDE.md content** | **Motivation:** Sessions 1–101 produced a fully operational paper trading system (T1 + T4 active for MLB; T4 NBA deployed), yet no session document stated *why the system exists* or *what commercial claim it is structured to prove*. As Earl noted: "Probably would've saved a lot of sessions." Three structural gaps were identified and filled this session. **Decision 1 — Principal vision:** `## Principal Vision` section added to CLAUDE.md before `## What This Project Is`. Canonical statement: "This is a calibration engine for probabilistic reasoning, structured as a trading bot." Prediction markets are the only widely available instrument providing all three simultaneously: binary-resolved outcomes (unambiguous right/wrong), short feedback loops (hours/days not quarters), and market-implied probability as an explicit benchmark. Corollary: "P&L is the score. The methodology is the asset." If the methodology proves edge rigorously, it generalizes. New sport = new C1+C2+C3, same methodology. New venue = new VenueAdapter, same methodology. **Decision 2 — Alpha taxonomy as first-class architectural concept:** Subsection `### Alpha taxonomy — two distinct theses, two independent proofs required` added inside `## What This Project Is`. Canonical framing: T1 alpha (pregame calibration edge) and T4 alpha (live market latency edge) are structurally independent hypotheses. T1 tests whether historical rolling statistics produce better-calibrated pregame probabilities than Kalshi markets. T4 tests whether markets are slow to incorporate in-game state changes at 45-90s polling cadence. A verdict on one does NOT transfer to the other. EXP-017 is the T1 validation track. GG-046 is the T4 validation track. The ~1,000 evaluations/game in T4 is a structural consequence of the hypothesis, not a target: 18 batters × 2 thresholds × ~480 cycles/game = ~17,000 evaluation slots; at 2-3% fire rate = 50-100 actual bets. **Decision 3 — Market efficiency gradient as binding operational constraint:** Subsection `### Market efficiency gradient — where the strategy is designed to work` added. The system is designed to exploit markets where pricing is inefficient. MLB mid-week regular season (low public attention, $500-$2,000 book depth, slower price discovery) is the target environment. NBA Finals player props (professional bettors, high public volume, enormous liquidity) are hostile conditions — the anti-predictive GG-037 finding was produced in worst-case efficiency conditions. Binding implication for expansion: the first question when adding a new market is not "do we have data?" but "is this market less efficient than a calibrated model can produce?" Additional finding: Kalshi KXMLBHIT depth sets a capital ceiling structural to the venue. At $10K+ bankroll, full Kelly sizing moves the market against the bot; this ceiling (GG-045) is not addressable by improved modeling. **Scope:** All three sections are session-read canonical CLAUDE.md content — read by every model session before any other work. Prior to this session, a model cold-starting on this codebase had no access to these framing concepts. | DS — Earl Ignacio 2026-06-29 SESSION 101 |
| 2026-06-29 | **[SESSION 101] SemanticFloorViolation (T1 MLB) + ContaminationGuard (T4 MLB) — runtime behavioral contracts deployed** | **Motivation (ADR-0034):** SESSION 100 accepted ADR-0034 (Layer 3 Semantic Contracts). SESSION 101 implements the first two contracts at the code level. **Contract 1 — SemanticFloorViolation (PIPELINE_FLOOR, `run_mlb_paper_trade.py`):** New `SemanticFloorViolation(RuntimeError)` class. New `_count_scheduled_mlb_games(game_date) → int` helper — counts Preview-state games via MLB Stats API; returns 0 on API failure (permissive). After the Kalshi market-matching block, if `len(markets_by_player) == 0`: calls `_count_scheduled_mlb_games()`; if count > 0, logs `PIPE-05 / SemanticFloorViolation` to C6 with payload (game_date, scheduled_preview_games, kalshi_markets_total, kalshi_markets_quoted), prints to stderr (greppable in cron log), exits with code 2. Guard only fires when `Preview`-state games exist — Final-only days (games complete, markets closed) and off-season (no games) correctly produce 0 count and no violation. T1 pipeline silence (ticker/name normalization failure) now surfaces within seconds of running. **Contract 2 — ContaminationGuard (EVALUATION_GATE, `run_t4_mlb_live.py`):** New `ContaminationGuard` class with 4 checks per evaluation: `game_state_fresh` (cycle_start_at within 2×POLL_INTERVAL_S=90s), `market_active` (pm.status=active and has_live_quotes=True), `player_in_game` (ls.player_id == player_id), `snapshot_not_stale` (snapshot_fetched_at within 90s). `check()` returns False on any failure and logs `COMPONENT / CONTAMINATION_HALT` to C6 audit JSONL with failed_checks, total_halts, staleness_halts. Staleness halts also emit `logger.warning()` for cron log visibility. `_run_cycle()` signature extended with `contamination_guard: Optional[ContaminationGuard]`, `cycle_start_at: Optional[datetime]`, `snapshot_fetched_at: Optional[datetime]` (all default None → backward-compatible with existing tests). Guard guards the `pm is None or not has_live_quotes` inline check, replacing the inline check when guard is present. `cycle_start_at` captured immediately before ESPN polling; `snapshot_fetched_at` immediately after Kalshi price fetch. `ContaminationGuard` instantiated once in `main()` (session-scoped). Cycle log now includes `cg_halts=N(stale=M)`. **CLAUDE.md Step 4 updated** with grep commands for both contracts. **Tests:** 13 new tests (5 SemanticFloorViolation, 8 ContaminationGuard). 144/144 for modified test suites. Zero regressions. | EL |
| 2026-06-29 | **[SESSION 101] ContaminationGuard naming collision resolution — local class renamed to `LiveDataFreshnessGuard`** | When wiring ADR-0034 canonical `ContaminationGuard` (`semantic_contracts.py`) into `run_t4_mlb_live.py`, Session 101 commit `cd4b663` had already introduced a local class also named `ContaminationGuard`. Import collision: cannot import canonical under the same name as the local class without aliasing everywhere. **Resolution:** Rename local class to `LiveDataFreshnessGuard` (communicates what it does: checks timestamp freshness). Canonical `ContaminationGuard` imported as `SemanticContaminationGuard` at the use site. **Two guards are complementary and co-exist in `_run_cycle()`:** `LiveDataFreshnessGuard` is session-scoped (one instance in `main()`), checks timestamp freshness via `.check(ticker, player_id, ls, pm, cycle_start_at, snapshot_fetched_at)`, has `total_halts`/`staleness_halts` counters. `SemanticContaminationGuard` (canonical `ContaminationGuard`) is per-evaluation (constructor-injected with all eval params), checks business-logic coherence via `.is_clean() → bool`, includes h_needed/ab_remaining math impossibility check. All 8 `TestContaminationGuard` test class references updated to `TestLiveDataFreshnessGuard`. `_run_cycle()` parameter type annotation updated. | EL |
| 2026-06-29 | **[SESSION 101] SemanticFloorViolation exception hierarchy: `RuntimeError` → `Exception` via `SemanticContractError`** | Session 101 commit `cd4b663` defined the local class as `SemanticFloorViolation(RuntimeError)`. Canonical ADR-0034 module defines `SemanticContractError(Exception)` as base and `SemanticFloorViolation(SemanticContractError)` as the leaf. **Why Exception not RuntimeError:** `RuntimeError` implies a Python interpreter failure (e.g., recursion limit exceeded). A floor violation is a governance/business-logic failure — the Python runtime is healthy; the pipeline is semantically broken. Domain-specific exception hierarchies must use `Exception` (or a domain base) to avoid misleading the exception class. **Impact on tests:** `test_run_mlb_paper_trade.py::test_violation_class_is_runtime_error` renamed to `test_violation_class_is_exception`; assertion updated from `issubclass(runner.SemanticFloorViolation, RuntimeError)` to `issubclass(runner.SemanticFloorViolation, Exception)`. After Patch 1 wires the canonical import, `runner.SemanticFloorViolation` resolves to the canonical `SemanticContractError` subclass — both assertions hold (`issubclass(SFV, Exception)` is True; `issubclass(SFV, RuntimeError)` is False). | EL |
| 2026-06-29 | **[SESSION 101] `assert_floor()` MLB Stats API call optimization — API only queried when `matched_players == 0`** | `SemanticFloorGuard.assert_floor()` requires `games_fetched: int` as input (it does not call the API itself — separation of concerns). The naive wiring in `run_mlb_paper_trade.py` would call `_count_scheduled_mlb_games(GAME_DATE_ISO)` (one MLB Stats API HTTP call) on every pipeline run to populate `games_fetched`. **Optimization:** Wrap the entire `assert_floor()` call block inside `if len(markets_by_player) == 0:`. On clean runs (players matched > 0), the API call is never made — zero latency overhead. The MLB Stats API is only queried when a floor violation is possible (matched_players == 0), which is the only condition under which `games_fetched` matters. **Result:** 0 added latency on clean pipeline runs. Floor violation detection incurs API overhead only when something may actually be wrong — the precise condition where spending a few hundred ms on diagnosis is worthwhile. Pattern: caller is responsible for lazily populating `games_fetched` and should do so only when `assert_floor()` can raise. | EL |
| 2026-06-25 | **[SESSION 87 cont.] MLB arm institutional completion — T4 bug fixes, T1/T3 scheduling, T2 build (DS-04 MLB)** | **T4 bugs found and fixed in `run_t4_mlb_live.py`:** (1) Stat group filter (`not in ("batting", "")` → `!= "batting"`) — the empty-string case allowed pitching/other stat groups with no name to be processed as batting, corrupting batter hit counts from non-batting stat groups. (2) `MIN_EDGE_T4 = 0.02` — dead constant never referenced in any filter (actual pre-filter was `gross_edge <= 0.0`; C4 gates at 0.04). Removed to eliminate misleading documentation; filter at `<= 0.0` preserved as-is. (3) NFR-03 compliance — kill switch checked only at top of `while True` loop; worst-case halt time with 15 live games × 10s ESPN timeout = 150s + 45s sleeve = 195s, violating NFR-03 ≤60s. Fix: kill switch check added inside ESPN game-poll `for` loop; if armed, set flag and `break`; outer loop re-checks immediately. Per-game halt time now ≤ one ESPN timeout (~10s max). **T1 timing (PIPE-05) fixed:** Changed `timedelta(days=1)` → `_now_utc.date()` (same-day UTC target). Cron moved from `30 23 * * *` to `00 3 * * *`. Rationale: Kalshi publishes KXMLBHIT markets by ~01:00 UTC; all ET-dated games (first pitch ≥17:05 UTC) are "Preview" at 03:00 UTC. S85 explicitly required these before re-enable; S86 breached this gate. **T2 MLB built (PIPE-05.5, `run_t2_mlb_sameday.py`):** Same-day batting lineup overlay. Runs at 19:30 UTC (3:30pm ET). DS-04 MLB designated (`03_DECISIONS/DS-04_mlb-t2-lineup-source.md`). Two adjustments: (1) lineup confirmation — players not in today's confirmed batting lineup (scratched/benched) are SCRATCHED and skipped; (2) Binomial slot adjustment — confirmed batting slot → expected ABs → ratio × p_t1 = p_t2 (capped ±15%). Per-game contamination guard: skips players whose game_pk is already Live (unlike T1's global guard — T2 is per-game to allow evening-game bets while afternoon games are live). Pool: shared `paper_portfolio_mlb.json` with T1; idempotency guard blocks re-entry on T1-open tickers. Cron: `30 19 * * *`. **T5 status:** Explicitly out of scope per CLAUDE.md ("Not built. Future; requires real-time infrastructure.") — no new entry needed. **Governance:** `MLBIngestionClient.fetch_lineups()` method added to `mlb_ingestion.py`. 29 new tests (`test_t191_mlb_t2.py`). `TestGameDateIsTomorrow` renamed `TestGameDateIsToday` with updated logic and dates. **Suite: 1947 passed, 0 failed.** | EL |

| 2026-06-30 | **[SESSION 102 / DS ruling] MLB YES-ONLY gate rescinded — `MLB_PAPER_YES_ONLY` set to `false` in both T1 and T2 cron entries** | **Decision authority:** DS sign-off Earl Ignacio, 2026-06-30 SESSION 102. **Gate being rescinded:** `MLB_PAPER_YES_ONLY=true` was a DS directive from SESSION 56c, mirroring the NBA paper-trading YES-ONLY gate while directional MLB edge data was absent. **Rationale for rescission:** The NBA paper record (158 resolved bets) produced a strongly anti-predictive finding: bets where `edge > 0` (model predicts YES) had a win rate of **0.356** vs. bets where `edge ≤ 0` had a win rate of **0.747** (GG-037). This establishes that the model over-estimates YES probability vs. Kalshi markets. Continuing to gate MLB bets to YES-only would (1) inject the same systematic directional bias that produced the NBA anti-predictive finding, (2) prevent EXP-017 from testing the commercial question with an unbiased directional sample, and (3) accumulate a deliberately biased MLB paper record that cannot serve as sound validation evidence. The NBA finding is proof that the YES-only gate causes harm to the validation dataset when calibration direction is unknown. **Affected cron entries (commit `cron.conf` Session 102):** T1 MLB (cron `0 11 * * *`): `MLB_PAPER_YES_ONLY=true` → `false`. T2 MLB (cron `30 19 * * *`): `MLB_PAPER_YES_ONLY=true` → `false`. **Effect on `run_mlb_paper_trade.py`:** `PAPER_YES_ONLY = os.environ.get("MLB_PAPER_YES_ONLY", "true").lower() == "true"` resolves to `False`; the NO-bet filter at line 855-856 no longer fires. Both YES and NO direction bets now eligible for MLB paper record. **ADR-0035 created** same session: NBA paper portfolio kill-switch resolution (reset at 2026-27 season start, October 2026). | EL — DS ruling Earl Ignacio 2026-06-30 SESSION 102 |
| 2026-06-30 | **[SESSION 103 / GG-052] T4 MLB staleness storm root cause: `cycle_start_at` captured before Kalshi paginated fetch — moved to after fetch** | **Root cause (GG-052):** June 30 T4 MLB produced 1632 `CONTAMINATION_HALT` events and only 2 bets. `LiveDataFreshnessGuard.game_state_fresh` check uses `(now - cycle_start_at).total_seconds() < _FRESHNESS_WINDOW_S` where `_FRESHNESS_WINDOW_S = 90s` (2 × `POLL_INTERVAL_S = 45s`). `cycle_start_at` was captured at the top of the outer polling loop, **before** both the ESPN polls and the Kalshi paginated fetch. `fetch_player_points_markets()` uses cursor pagination (page_size=200, 3+ pages for 500+ KXMLBHIT markets) with exponential retry backoff (`RETRY_DELAY_S=2.0` → delays 4+8+16+32=60s per page under throttling). Total Kalshi fetch time: 60–130+ seconds per cycle under normal rate-limiting. By the time `_run_cycle()` started, `(now - cycle_start_at)` was already 90–130s — exceeding the 90s window — causing ALL `game_state_fresh` checks to fail immediately. **Diagnostic evidence:** Audit JSONL showed all 28+ `CONTAMINATION_HALT` entries per cycle firing at the exact same timestamp (e.g., all at 22:37:37). `_run_cycle()` is sub-second for 72 evaluations; a sub-second burst of halts proves the window expired before `_run_cycle()` was entered, not during it. C0017 was the one cycle with evaluations: Kalshi fetch happened to take ~86s (just under 90s), allowing 50 evals to pass before the window expired 4s later. **Fix:** Removed `cycle_start_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)` from before the ESPN polls. Added `cycle_start_at = snapshot_fetched_at` immediately after `snapshot_fetched_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)` (post-Kalshi fetch). `snapshot_fetched_at` and `cycle_start_at` now share the same timestamp. The 90s freshness window starts after data is fully loaded, not before the 60–130s fetch. Commit `bbaf86a`. VPS deployed, t4-mlb service restarted. **Secondary issue registered (T-209):** Two concurrent T4 sessions (CE494B3A from 06:30 UTC + B7193041 from 22:05 UTC) were both hitting the Kalshi API, doubling rate-limit pressure — amplifying the staleness burn. Investigation pending. | EL |
| 2026-07-01 | **[SESSION 104 / DS ruling] NBA component work tabled until ~1 month before 2026-27 season tip-off — MLB sole active development priority** | **Operator directive (Earl Ignacio, 2026-07-01):** "Table any NBA component work until we get 1 month away from the NBA season. We're focused on what's actually live right now — and that market is the MLB." **Scope:** No new builds, fixes, or active investigation on any NBA component — T4 NBA (`run_t4_live.py`, `t4_live_runner.py`, `t4_price_cache.py`), T1/T2/T3 NBA, NBA C1/C2/C3, GG-056 (NBA drawdown/kill-switch), or ADR-0035 (NBA portfolio reset) implementation work — until approximately one month before NBA 2026-27 regular-season tip-off. Exact date not yet fixed — must be checked against the official published 2026-27 NBA schedule when it becomes relevant; do not assume a specific date. **Does not rescind or alter ADR-0035** (NBA portfolio reset at season start remains ACCEPTED and will execute when the season starts regardless of this directive) or GG-056 (remains OPEN, un-superseded — this directive broadens its practical effect from "the drawdown question is deferred" to "all NBA engineering is deferred"). **Registered in:** `04_EXECUTION/TODO_NEXT.md` (standing directive section ahead of SESSION 103 entries) and project memory (`project_nba_deferred.md`) so any future session is informed without re-prompting. | EL — DS ruling Earl Ignacio 2026-07-01 SESSION 104 |

| 2026-07-01 | **[SESSION 106 / GG-058] T4 MLB UTC-midnight rollover fixed — merge-in replaces destructive replace, closing the nightly in-progress-game drop** | **Root cause confirmed exactly as GG-058 documented:** `run_t4_mlb_live.py`'s date-rollover block ran `game_ids[:] = _espn_mlb_game_ids_today()` at every UTC-midnight crossing, replacing the full tracked game list with the new calendar day's ESPN slate. Since ESPN's `dates=` scoreboard query returns only that day's games, any West-coast game still live at UTC midnight (common — first pitch 22:30–01:45 UTC straddles the boundary) was silently dropped and never polled again, directly suppressing the CLAUDE.md ~1,000-evals/game throughput target for every late game, every night. **Fix (matches GG-058's own resolution path):** Added `_espn_game_status(espn_game_id) -> str` — single-game ESPN status lookup, returns `""` on fetch failure (a failure is never treated as terminal, so a transient ESPN outage cannot cause a false drop). Added `_rollover_game_ids(tracked_game_ids, newly_discovered, status_fn=_espn_game_status) -> list[str]` — pure function implementing merge-in: carries forward any tracked game not yet in `newly_discovered` and not at `STATUS_FINAL`/`STATUS_POSTPONED`, then appends new-day discoveries. Main loop now does `game_ids[:] = _rollover_game_ids(game_ids, newly_discovered)` instead of the destructive replace. **Secondary fix found during implementation:** pregame-context dicts (`by_player_id`, `name_to_pregame`) were `.clear()`'d before `.update()` at rollover — combined with `_load_pregame_context()` only ever loading the single most-recent day's file, this could silently drop player-matching context for carried-over games. Changed to additive `.update()` only; accepted that these dicts now only grow (judged acceptable — small per-player payloads, daily service restart regardless). **Test coverage:** `TestEspnGameStatus` (3 tests) + `TestRolloverGameIds` (7 tests) added to `tests/test_run_t4_mlb_live.py`, including the safety-critical case (fetch failure treated as non-terminal, not a drop trigger). Full suite: 2159 passed, 2 pre-existing unrelated failures (stale Kalshi test-market dates, stale feature CSV — both pre-existing freshness checks, not regressions). **Registered T-215** for this fix. Commit and VPS deploy to follow this entry. | EL |
| 2026-07-01 | **[SESSION 106 / GG-062] Dual concurrent `t4-mlb` processes recurred for ~1h48m — T-210's `pkill`-based singleton guard proven race-prone** | **Finding:** While investigating a "double-rollover, 28s apart" anomaly flagged in the SESSION 105 handoff, forensic analysis of `t4_mlb_live_cron.log` proved two independent `run_t4_mlb_live.py` processes ran concurrently from ~23:31 UTC 2026-06-30 through 01:19:19 UTC 2026-07-01 (~1h48m) — the same failure class T-210 (commit `670b88b`) was built to eliminate. **Evidence:** `cycle_n` is monotonic-only by construction (incremented once per completed cycle, no reset path); the log showed non-monotonic cycle numbers interleaved (`cycle=0029` followed by `cycle=0028`). Isolating the full window resolved two distinct series: one climbing `cg_halts` 878→1632 with `eval=50`/`fired=2`; an orphan permanently flat at `cg_halts=0`/`eval=0`/`fired=0`. `journalctl -u t4-mlb` showed systemd restarting the unit twice, 10 seconds apart, at Jun 30 06:30:35 and 06:30:45 — the probable origin: the second restart's `ExecStartPre=-pkill` raced the first restart's not-yet-registered new process. The orphan survived undetected for ~19 hours until the next restart (01:19:19 UTC) incidentally cleared it. Currently only one PID is alive (confirmed via `systemctl status`/`pgrep`) — the orphan is gone, but nothing caught it while it ran; this was only found via manual log forensics, not any automated signal. **Why this matters:** `ExecStartPre=-/usr/bin/pkill -f run_t4_mlb_live.py` is a best-effort, race-prone pattern match — it depends on timing between a dying process and process-name deregistration, not an atomic lock. This incident is direct proof the mechanism can fail under back-to-back restarts. **Impact:** MEDIUM-HIGH — paper-capital-only exposure (no live capital), but double-counting risk against the shared portfolio ledger for the full window, and a demonstrated blind spot in the project's own operational health check (Step 4 only shows the current PID, not historical overlaps). **Resolution path registered as GG-062 / T-216:** replace the `pkill`-based guard with a proper PID-file lock (atomic check-and-write, refuses second start rather than best-effort cleanup of the prior one), and add a `DUAL_SESSION_DETECTED` audit event if two `session_id`s are ever active in the same window, so this failure class self-reports instead of requiring manual forensics. Not yet implemented — flagged OPEN, MEDIUM-HIGH priority, ahead of the next redeploy/restart cycle (including this session's own GG-058 deploy). | EL |
| 2026-07-01 | **[SESSION 106] T1 MLB "50-bet anomaly" investigated and closed — not a bug; direct consequence of the SESSION 102 YES-ONLY gate rescission** | **Task #2 of this session's sequential queue.** The reported jump (2 bets → 50 bets across consecutive PIPE-05 runs) was first mis-framed as same-day consecutive runs; reconstructing `paper_mlb_cron.log` run headers showed the two runs were actually **2026-06-30** (2 bets, 128 player/threshold pairs evaluated) and **2026-07-01** (50 bets, 99 pairs evaluated) — different calendar days. The header block for each run carries a `Gate:` line: 06-30 shows `Gate: YES-ONLY ACTIVE`, 07-01 shows `Gate: YES-ONLY OFF`. This directly matches the DS-ruled YES-ONLY gate rescission (`MLB_PAPER_YES_ONLY` true→false, this same DECISION_LOG's SESSION 102 entry above) — the 06-30 11:00 UTC cron run executed before that day's rescission deploy landed, making it the last run under the old gate; 07-01 is the first full day under the new gate. Under the old gate only YES-direction bets could pass the filter (2 of 128 pairs had positive YES edge); with the gate off, both directions become eligible, explaining the jump to 50 of 99 pairs. **Ruled out a duplicate/re-bet bug layered on top** (per the GG-050 precedent of a prior T4 re-betting bug from an improperly-scoped dedup set): queried `paper_portfolio_mlb.json` for the 50 2026-07-01 positions by `(kalshi_ticker, kalshi_threshold, direction)` — **50 unique combinations, zero duplicates.** The 50-bet count is fully explained by the gate rescission alone; no additional bug found. **Verdict: not a bug. Closed.** No code change required. | EL |
| 2026-07-01 | **[SESSION 107 / T-212] Data quality contract manifest implemented as typed Python module (`data_contracts.py`), not literal YAML (GG-059's original text)** | **GG-059 registered "data_contracts.yaml" as its illustrative naming; this session evaluated that literally versus the codebase's actual conventions before building.** **Why not YAML:** (1) No YAML infra exists anywhere in the project — `pyproject.toml` dependencies (pandas, numpy, scikit-learn, scipy, jsonlines, python-dotenv, cryptography, requests) do not include `pyyaml`, and `06_CODE_EXECUTION_REPRODUCIBILITY/DEPENDENCY_LOCKS/requirements-base.txt` has no pinned entry — adding it is a real infra change (pip install + lock-file update + VPS install), disproportionate for a ~40-field manifest. (2) `pyproject.toml` enforces mypy **strict** mode project-wide (`disallow_untyped_defs`, `disallow_any_generics`, `no_implicit_reexport`, `warn_return_any`) — a YAML manifest loaded at runtime is untyped by construction (`dict[str, Any]` at best) and would be the only unchecked data-shape surface in an otherwise fully-typed codebase. (3) Direct precedent already exists for "constraint manifest as typed Python," not config file: `_HARD_LIMITS` in `c4_risk_engine/risk.py`, `_MLB_BATTER_COLUMNS`/`_MLB_PITCHER_COLUMNS` in `c1_signal_ingestion/mlb_ingestion.py`. A YAML file would be a new, inconsistent pattern alongside these. **Design:** `data_contracts.py` defines frozen `FieldContract`/`SourceContract` dataclasses and three contract instances (`MLB_BATTER_CONTRACT`, `MLB_PITCHER_CONTRACT`, `MLB_FEATURE_CSV_CONTRACT`), importing `_MLB_BATTER_COLUMNS`/`_MLB_PITCHER_COLUMNS` directly from `mlb_ingestion.py` (one-directional import, no cycle) rather than hand-duplicating the column lists — schema drift between C1's actual output and the contract is structurally prevented rather than relying on manual sync. **Scope note:** intent of GG-059 (declarative, auditable, drift-checkable schema manifest, checked at C1 and C2 boundaries) is fully preserved; only the file format changed. | EL |
| 2026-07-01 | **[SESSION 106 / GG-063] 100% of all MLB Kalshi demo order submissions have failed since inception — surfaced as a byproduct of the Task #2 duplicate-check** | **Discovery context:** While pulling `paper_portfolio_mlb.json` records to check for duplicate tickers (Task #2 above), every sampled record showed `"kalshi_order_status": "failed: HTTP 410"`. Widened to a full-history census: **T1 MLB — 52/52 positions ever recorded, 100% `failed: HTTP 410`, zero successes.** **T4 MLB — 15/15 positions ever recorded, 100% `failed: HTTP 410`, zero successes.** For comparison, **NBA T1 on the identical shared code path** (`kalshi_client.submit_order()`) shows a 73% real-confirmation rate (`resting`/`executed` = 115 of 158) — proving the failure is MLB-series-specific (`KXMLBHIT`), not a credentials/signing/shared-retry-path problem. HTTP 410 ("Gone") is a distinct failure class from the previously-known GG-014 429/503 transient-rate-limit pattern; it is not covered by the existing `(429, 503)` retry logic and does not self-resolve on retry. Root cause is **not yet isolated**: the exact same `mkt.ticker` string is confirmed open/listed by the live `GET /markets?series_ticker=KXMLBHIT&status=open` DS-02 fetch moments before `submit_order()` is called with it — yet the order POST is rejected as Gone. The actual Kalshi response body is silently discarded before reaching the audit log (`kalshi_client.py`'s `_post()` captures `r.text[:500]` but `submit_order()`'s error path only forwards the bare `f"HTTP {r.status_code}"` string), so the real rejection reason has never been recorded anywhere. **Why this was invisible until now:** ADR-0008 designed the local ledger as authoritative and the bot as non-halting on Kalshi failure (paper capital unaffected) — so a 100% external-confirmation failure rate produces zero visible symptoms in bankroll, P&L, or bet-count metrics. The SESSION 95 DECISION_LOG entry claiming T4 MLB Kalshi wiring was "Verified" confirmed only that an order *attempt* was made (`budget_used` incrementing), not that any order was *accepted* — same failure shape as GG-060 (Silent Success Indistinguishability). **Impact:** HIGH evidentiary / ZERO capital (paper mode, local ledger remains G6-authoritative per ADR-0008). But ADR-0008's stated purpose — external Kalshi-side confirmation strengthening G6 evidence quality via "two-level confirmation" — has never been achieved for any MLB paper bet, in either band, for the entire history of MLB paper trading. Any future G6 sign-off narrative must not cite MLB Kalshi-side confirmation as evidence until this is resolved. **Resolution path registered as GG-063 / T-217:** (1) thread the real response body through to `kalshi_order_status`/the audit payload so the next attempt reveals Kalshi's actual rejection reason; (2) isolate whether this is a market-vs-event ticker mismatch, a KXMLBHIT-specific order-eligibility rule, or a demo-key MLB-series permissions gap; (3) re-verify with a deliberate manual test order once root cause is hypothesized. **Not fixed this session** — diagnostic-only finding, consistent with the sequential-completion directive (byproduct of Task #2, same pattern as GG-062 earlier this session). | EL |
| 2026-07-01 | **[SESSION 106 / GG-057] Retroactive governance entry — C8 web dashboard (`web_dashboard.py`/`pmbot-web-dashboard.service`/`Open Dashboard.command`) built and deployed SESSION 104 without a paper trail** | **Task #3 of this session's sequential queue.** This entry supplies resolution-path step (2) of GG-057: a retroactive record of a decision that was already made and already shipped. **What was built (SESSION 104, prior to this entry):** `scripts/web_dashboard.py` — an ~868-line dependency-free stdlib (`http.server`) HTTP server that reads `paper_portfolio.json`/`paper_portfolio_mlb.json`/`paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` and the audit JSONL logs directly off disk and renders them as a human-readable status page. No API calls, no secrets, no write path — pure read/render. Built to replace a curses TUI (`run_mlb_dashboard.py`, T-188) that crashed on SSH disconnect, which was the original C8 viewing mechanism. `scripts/pmbot-web-dashboard.service` — a hardened systemd unit binding to `127.0.0.1:8050` only (not reachable off-box), with `PrivateTmp=true`, `NoNewPrivileges=true`, `ReadOnlyPaths=` scoped to the logs directory only, plus `MemoryMax`/`CPUQuota` resource caps. `Open Dashboard.command` — a local double-clickable launcher that opens an SSH tunnel (`ssh -L 8050:127.0.0.1:8050 prediction-market-vps`) and then a browser tab, so the dashboard is viewable from Earl's machine without ever exposing the port beyond the VPS loopback interface. **Why this matters as a governance entry, not just a commit message:** CLAUDE.md hard rule #7 requires every strategic decision to hit DECISION_LOG.md immediately, not at session end — this component shipped to production (confirmed live via `systemctl status` since 2026-07-01 00:54:58 UTC) with zero DECISION_LOG entry, zero GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG mention, and zero MEMORY_BANK/TODO_NEXT reference at the time, until GG-057 caught it in SESSION 104 by diffing untracked files against governance logs. The three files were already committed to git in commit `b247cf2` (SESSION 104) — that commit's message claimed to "resolve" GG-057, but resolution-path steps (2) DECISION_LOG entry and (3) MEMORY_BANK line were never actually done; this entry and the accompanying MEMORY_BANK.md "Last reviewed" update (SESSION 106) close that gap. **Verified safe by design, not just by claim:** read-only, localhost-bound, no secrets, hardened systemd sandboxing — the engineering was sound; the failure was purely procedural (a real deploy with no paper trail). **Status: GG-057 marked RESOLVED in GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md as of this entry.** | EL |
| 2026-07-02 | **[SESSION 108 / T-212] Regression closed: 11 fixtures expanded to the 44-col contract, `TestFreshnessGuardInSourceCode` retired as dead-code assertions — plus 2 pre-existing failures the SESSION 107 handoff never diagnosed, found and fixed as a byproduct** | **Resume context:** SESSION 107 paused at context limit with 17 failing tests, fully diagnosed per its handoff (`SESSION_HANDOFF_20260702_0045.md`) into two classes: Class 1 (11 tests, synthetic fixtures using a handful of columns against the new 24/44-col `data_contracts.py` schema) and Class 2 (6 tests, `test_t193_mlb_freshness_guard.py::TestFreshnessGuardInSourceCode` grep-on-source assertions for `_FRESHNESS_WARN_DAYS`/`_FRESHNESS_HALT_DAYS`/`feature_csv_stale_{warn,halt}` — constants T-212's `assert_contract()` intentionally removed). **Verification before acting:** re-ran the full suite before touching anything, per this session's own governance-scan discipline (measure before cutting) — confirmed 17 failed, but the handoff's Class 1 list only named 9 tests (3 `test_generate_mlb_features.py` + 1 `test_run_mlb_paper_trade.py` + 5 `test_run_mlb_pregame.py`), not 11. The 2-test gap was `tests/integration/test_mlb_api_connectivity.py::test_ticker_dates_are_current_or_future` and `tests/integration/test_mlb_pipeline_smoke.py::test_feature_csv_freshness` — never mentioned in the handoff at all, and confirmed via the SESSION 106 DECISION_LOG entry (GG-058 row) to be pre-existing failures predating T-212 entirely, not part of its regression. Isolated and diagnosed both independently: (1) `test_ticker_dates_are_current_or_future` had its own bug — `datetime.strptime(..., "%d%b%y")` against a regex capture order of `(YY)(MON)(DD)`, producing e.g. ticker `26JUL02` (2026-07-02) parsed as day=26/year=2002; fixed the format string to `"%y%b%d"` to match capture order. (2) `test_feature_csv_freshness` is real stale local data (`exp_output/MLB/mlb_feature_matrix.csv`, 8 days old) in a gitignored, manually-run integration fixture (`pytestmark = pytest.mark.integration`, real network calls, documented "never run in standard CI" though no `-m` deselect is actually wired into `pyproject.toml`'s `addopts`) — regenerating it requires deleting the cached 2026-season parquet and re-pulling live MLB Stats API data, a disproportionate, open-ended-duration action for a fixture-freshness fix; left open and reported to operator rather than run unprompted. **Class 1 fix:** `sample_batter_df` (`test_generate_mlb_features.py`) gained `team_id`/`ingestion_run_id`/`ingested_at`; `test_multiple_seasons_combined`'s inline 3-col mock replaced with the fixed fixture; `test_run_mlb_paper_trade.py::test_exits_0_when_no_markets` and `test_run_mlb_pregame.py::_make_feature_csv` (shared helper, 5 dependent tests) rebuilt to the full 44-column `MLB_FEATURE_CSV_CONTRACT` schema, preserving all previously-asserted values (`player_id=660670`, `player_name="Juan Soto"`, `ba_l10=0.280`, `days_rest=1`, `h_l10_mean=1.0`, `game_date` offsets). **Class 2 fix:** `TestFreshnessGuardInSourceCode` (6 tests) deleted from `test_t193_mlb_freshness_guard.py` with a comment explaining why and where its behavioral coverage re-homes (`test_validators.py`/`test_data_contracts.py`, not yet written); confirmed via full method inventory that the file's other five classes (`Fresh`/`Warning`/`Halt`/`NoDates`/`Thresholds`) test a locally-mirrored pure function, not production source text, so they are unaffected and retained. Removed the now-unused `Path` import; left `sys`/`StringIO`/`MagicMock`/`patch` (already unused pre-existing) untouched — out of scope. **Result:** `pytest tests/ -q` → 1 failed (the stale-CSV item above, tracked separately, not a regression), 2155 passed, 6 skipped. Down from 17 failures at pause. **Also found in the working tree, left untouched:** an uncommitted diagnostic fix in `kalshi_client.py` (GG-063/T-217 error-body threading) from a separate session — staged and committed as its own file, not folded into this entry's commit. | EL |
| 2026-07-01 | **[SESSION 107 / T-212] `validators.py`'s `assert_contract()` owns its own C6 logging internally, diverging from the `SemanticFloorGuard.assert_floor()` precedent** | **Precedent being diverged from:** `c7_monitoring/semantic_contracts.py`'s `SemanticFloorGuard.assert_floor()` explicitly does NOT call `audit.log()` itself — its docstring states the guard has no knowledge of C6 configuration and all C6 writes must flow through the component that owns the C6 instance; the caller catches `SemanticFloorViolation` and logs it. **Why `assert_contract()` does the opposite:** T-212/GG-059 exists specifically to *replace* three separate duplicated ad hoc schema/freshness-check-and-log blocks (in `scripts/generate_mlb_features.py`, `run_mlb_paper_trade.py`, `run_mlb_pregame.py`) with one shared implementation. If `assert_contract()` did not also own the C6 write, each of the three call sites would still need to hand-roll their own payload-shape logging after catching `DataContractViolation` — perpetuating the exact duplication this task exists to eliminate. `assert_contract()` is constructed with an `AuditLogger` instance passed explicitly by the caller (same ownership model as `SemanticFloorGuard` — it does not construct or configure C6 itself, it is only handed one), so the "no knowledge of C6 configuration" concern from the `assert_floor()` precedent does not apply; only the "who calls `.log()`" behavior differs. **NFR-03 tie-in:** if `emit_validation_result()`'s C6 write fails, `assert_contract()` treats that failure itself as an additional HALT-severity condition appended to the raised `DataContractViolation` — an unaudited pass is never trusted silently, matching the `all_ok`-never-flips-back-to-true pattern already used in `risk.py`. **Net effect:** the two frameworks now have deliberately different call-owns-logging policies for good reason — `semantic_contracts.py` is a shared library called from many bespoke contexts where centralizing the log call would obscure per-caller context; `data_contracts.py`/`validators.py` is a single generic contract-checking utility invoked identically from exactly three pipeline entrypoints, where centralizing the log call *removes* duplication instead of hiding context. | EL |
| 2026-07-02 | **[SESSION 108 / T-213 Phase 1] GG-060 C4 Edge Accounting built: per-evaluation edge-decision sink + run-level `summarize_edge_run()` classifier, wired live into T1 MLB** | **Problem being solved (GG-060):** a 0-bet paper-trading run is currently indistinguishable across three fundamentally different causes — (a) pipeline broken (0 evaluations ran at all), (b) pipeline healthy but genuinely no edge found (0 of N evaluations had `edge_signal`), (c) edge found but every candidate was risk-rejected (kill switch, exposure cap, size floor). CLAUDE.md's ~1,000-evals/game throughput mandate makes this ambiguity acute: at that volume, a silently-broken pipeline and a correctly-functioning-but-quiet one look identical from the outside. **Design — additive, zero-regression:** `RiskEngine.__init__` gained one new opt-in constructor parameter, `edge_logger: AuditLogger | None = None`, defaulting to inactive so all 50 pre-existing `test_c4_risk.py` tests needed no changes. The original `evaluate_forecast()` body (all phase-gate/8-CHECK/approval logic, ~430 lines, untouched internally) was renamed to `_evaluate_forecast_impl()`; a new thin public `evaluate_forecast()` wraps it and calls a new `_emit_edge_decision()` after every call — approved, rejected, or kill-switched alike — so a single emission point covers all ~9 early-return branches without touching any of their internal logic. Each edge-decision record captures `model_p`, `market_implied_p`, `vig`, `edge`, a boolean `edge_signal` (`abs(edge) >= MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE`), `recommended_direction`, `approval_status`, `rejection_reason`, a combined `decision` string, and `approved_size`. **Sink ownership pattern:** followed the existing `run_t4_mlb_live.py` precedent (`_AUDIT_LOG_PATH` = caller-owned plain `AuditLogger` instance, not baked into C4 itself) rather than hardcoding a default path in `risk.py` — keeps C4 sport-agnostic per CLAUDE.md ("C4... sport-agnostic. Governs capital regardless of what C3 is forecasting"). Wired into `run_mlb_paper_trade.py`: new `edge_logger = AuditLogger(LOG_DIR / "audit_mlb_edge_decisions.jsonl", min_severity="INFO")` constructed alongside the existing primary `audit_mlb.jsonl` logger, passed to both `RiskEngine(...)` instantiation sites (initial construction and the post-approved-bet engine rebuild — two genuinely distinct call sites at different indentation levels, confirmed via direct read, not a single find/replace). **Failure isolation:** a write failure on the edge sink is logged as a WARNING (`edge_decision_write_failed`) on the *primary* C6 logger, not treated as a kill-switch-triggering failure — this is a secondary analytics sink, not the canonical NFR-03 audit trail; `evaluate_forecast()`'s `ok` return value is unaffected by edge-sink write failures. **Run-level classifier:** new module function `summarize_edge_run(edge_log_path, session_id=None)` reads the JSONL sink back and classifies the run as `PIPELINE_BROKEN` (0 evaluations), `NO_EDGE` (evaluations ran, 0 had `edge_signal`), `RISK_REJECTED` (edge signals existed, 0 approved), or `ACTIVE` (>=1 approved) — plus a `silent_success_flag` (true when >=50 evaluations produced zero edge signals) and a `rejection_reason_counts` aggregation, directly implementing GG-060 resolution-path items (1)-(3). **Known gap, not yet fixed:** `run_mlb_paper_trade.py`'s forecast-record dict does not currently populate a `vig` key (unlike `run_mlb_pregame.py`, which does) — edge-decision records emitted from the live T1 pipeline will log `vig: null` until this is added; flagged for Phase 2, not blocking. **Not yet wired:** `run_t4_mlb_live.py`, `run_t2_mlb_sameday.py` (SKELETON), and EXP harnesses do not yet construct/pass an `edge_logger` — explicitly scoped as future phases (2)-(5) of GG-060's resolution path (silent-success detector activation, 7-day edge-rate trend, EXP-017/EXP-T4-01 retrospective template), not this session's scope. **Test coverage:** 16 new tests added to `tests/test_c4_risk.py` (`TestEdgeDecisionEmission`: backward-compat default, emission on approval/insufficient-edge-rejection/phase-gate-closed/kill-switch, vig pass-through, write-failure isolation; `TestSummarizeEdgeRun`: missing/empty file, NO_EDGE/RISK_REJECTED/ACTIVE classification, silent-success flag at the 50-evaluation boundary (49 vs 50), session_id filtering, rejection-reason aggregation) — all real end-to-end JSONL round-trips via `tmp_path` + a real `AuditLogger` instance, matching this file's existing convention of testing actual behavior over mocks. **Verification:** `test_c4_risk.py` 50 -> 66 passed (16 new, 0 regressed); `test_c4_risk.py` + `test_run_mlb_paper_trade.py` combined 97 passed; full suite `pytest tests/ -q` -> 2171 passed, 6 skipped, 1 failed (the pre-existing, already-documented `test_feature_csv_freshness` staleness failure from the T-212 entry above — unrelated, not a regression). **mypy strict:** run for the first time this session (see GG-065, newly registered) — confirmed zero new errors traceable to any T-213 code; all findings are pre-existing baseline debt (missing `py.typed` markers project-wide, missing `pandas-stubs`, unrelated pre-existing dict/list generic-argument omissions). **Registers T-213 Phase 1 as code-complete.** Phase 2 (vig field fix, T4/T2/EXP wiring, silent-success alerting, trend report, retrospective template) remains open. | EL |
| 2026-07-01 | **[SESSION 108 / GG-063 / T-217] True root cause of 100% MLB Kalshi order failure isolated: Kalshi hard-deprecated the v1 order endpoint, not a ticker/eligibility/permissions issue — `submit_order()` migrated to the v2 endpoint and empirically verified against DEMO** | **What the SESSION 106 discovery got wrong (understandably — the response body was never surfaced):** GG-063 registered three candidate hypotheses for the HTTP 410 — ticker/event mismatch, KXMLBHIT-specific eligibility rule, demo-key permissions gap. This session's mandate was to thread the real Kalshi response body through `submit_order()`'s error path first, per the resolution path, before hypothesizing further. Did so: `_post()` already captured `r.text[:500]` internally but `submit_order()`'s error branch discarded it behind `str(data.get("error", data))`; fixed to surface `data.get("body")`/`data.get("detail")` as well. **Actual response body once surfaced:** `deprecated_v1_order_endpoint` — Kalshi has hard-deprecated `POST /portfolio/orders` (v1). This is a platform-wide endpoint retirement, not a KXMLBHIT-specific or demo-key-specific condition. **Why NBA showed 73% success on the "identical" code path (per the SESSION 106 entry) while MLB showed 0%:** not a code-path difference — a timing one. NBA's 158 T1 paper bets were placed before Kalshi enforced the v1 deprecation; MLB paper trading began after enforcement, so every single MLB order hit the now-dead endpoint. Confirmed via the GG-063 entry's own NBA status breakdown: `{'resting': 87, 'executed': 28, 'failed: HTTP 503': 18, 'failed: HTTP 429': 16, 'failed: transient...': 9}` — zero HTTP 410s in the NBA history, consistent with NBA orders predating enforcement. **Fix (touches ADR-0008, immutable — superseding addendum to follow before session close per operator-selected option):** migrated `submit_order()` from `POST /portfolio/orders` (v1: `type`, `action`, `side=yes/no`, integer `count`, integer-cents `yes_price`/`no_price`) to `POST /portfolio/events/orders` (v2: single `side=bid/ask` field collapsing yes/no+buy/sell per Kalshi's published legacy→v2 mapping — `side="yes"`→`bid`, `side="no"`→`ask`, since this bot only ever buys — plus fixed-point decimal strings for `count`/`price` instead of ints, and two new required fields, `time_in_force="good_till_canceled"` and `self_trade_prevention_type="taker_at_cross"`). Schema verified against `docs.kalshi.com` across 5 independent WebFetch calls (create-order-v2 page x2, orders index, llms.txt index, order_direction.md migration guide) before writing code, given the risk of a wrong-direction bet if the yes/no→bid/ask mapping were misread. **Empirical verification (per operator's explicit "must verify with a manual test order" instruction):** wrote a one-off diagnostic script, ran a real order submission against Kalshi DEMO for a live-sourced KXMLBHIT ticker. Result: error type changed from `410 deprecated_v1_order_endpoint` (pre-fix) to `409 market_closed` (post-fix) — proof Kalshi now parses and evaluates the v2 request rather than rejecting it at the endpoint level. Could not obtain a full 200/201 confirmation this session: all 60 currently-open KXMLBHIT markets were for 2026-07-02 games (today is 2026-07-01) and DEMO's `open_time` for those markets had not yet passed (DEMO showed `status=initialized`, LIVE showed `status=active`, same ticker, at test time) — assessed as a timing artifact of testing outside a live game window, not a structural bug, but registered as a new open item requiring confirmation on the next live T1/T4 MLB cron run rather than further synthetic testing. **Also disproven before the real fix was attempted:** the original ticker-mismatch hypothesis — a controlled `GET /markets/{ticker}` against both DEMO and LIVE for the same ticker returned HTTP 200 on both, ruling out a demo-order-book/ticker-existence gap. **Test evidence:** `pytest tests/test_kalshi_client.py tests/test_t164_order_throttle.py -q` → 52/52 passed (no test calls `submit_order()` directly — a coverage gap, not addressed this session); full suite `pytest -q` → 2155 passed, 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (stale integration fixture, SESSION 108/T-212 territory), 6 skipped — no regressions from this change. **Scope discipline:** touched only `kalshi_client.py`; did not touch `data_contracts.py`/`validators.py` or the SESSION 107/108 pipeline test files per operator's explicit parallel-session boundary. | EL |
| 2026-07-02 | **[SESSION 108 / T-218] ADR-0039 drafted: MLB-native T4 sub-band architecture, scoping only, no code authorized** | Per operator session-open directive ("T-211 is high priority but requires a new task number and its own ADR... scope MLB-native T4 sub-bands before writing code"), registered **T-218** (next available task number after T-217) and drafted **ADR-0039**. Confirmed by direct read of `run_t4_mlb_live.py` that MLB T4 is a single flat evaluator (one Binomial model, `KELLY_T4 = 0.10`, one 45s poll loop) — the same "monolithic T4" problem ADR-0029 diagnosed for NBA, never fixed for MLB because MLB T4 was built independently (T-190/T-203/T-204) without inheriting the sub-band redesign. ADR-0039 does **not** copy the NBA roster verbatim — it first establishes four structural differences that change the architecture: (1) MLB C1 is poll-only (45s ESPN summary diff), not event-push, unlike NBA's assumed live feed; (2) MLB's core signal (a resolved at-bat) is a deterministic state transition on the resolution variable itself, not a probabilistic nudge like an NBA field goal; (3) pitcher identity/platoon split is an MLB-unique information class with no NBA analogue; (4) PA frequency is slot-gated and sparse (~3.1-4.2/game), organized around innings/time-through-order rather than a continuous clock. Proposes a 10-sub-band Tier B roster (T4M-PROJ [existing flat model, reclassified], T4M-EVENT, T4M-PITCHER, T4M-LINEUP, T4M-FLOW, T4M-BOUNDARY, T4M-ARITH, T4M-STAGED, T4M-CORR, T4M-EXIT) under the same two-tier acceptance model ADR-0029 used (Tier A architecture frozen on sign-off, Tier B roster/numerics provisional pending EXP per sub-band). Adopts ADR-0029's three-layer architecture (continuous eval → staleness-arbitrated queue → C5/C6) and Principles 1-6 unchanged as sport-agnostic; flags the ≤5s kill-switch SLA as requiring MLB-specific re-derivation rather than inheritance, since it was derived from NBA's continuous per-second event density. **New governance gap registered: GG-066** — MLB C1 has no real-time/sub-poll-interval event feed, blocking T4M-EVENT, T4M-LINEUP, and real-time T4M-ARITH firing until a new data source (candidate: MLB Stats API live game feed) is built; T4M-BOUNDARY and T4M-ARITH (poll-driven) require no new data source and are flagged as the cheapest next build. Also flagged (not registered as a gap): the existing flat model's `_AB_BY_SLOT` baseline is 9-inning-only and understates `ab_remaining` in extra innings — a candidate low-effort fix independent of this ADR. **No code authorized.** T-218 is scoping-only; each sub-band requires its own data-prerequisite resolution, EXP, and Phase 8 Activation Gate before implementation, per GG-061's process fix applied prospectively. | EL |
| 2026-07-02 | **[SESSION 108 CONTINUATION] ADR-0039 Status corrected ACCEPTED → DRAFT; governance-doc changes committed (`b001441`) after reconciling two previously-unseen parallel-session commits** | **Judgment call (made without live operator confirmation, per this segment's no-further-questions constraint):** ADR-0039 as drafted carried an internal contradiction — its Status line read "ACCEPTED (scoping only)" while its own DS Sign-Off section read "Pending." No live exchange with Earl Ignacio occurred to ratify it. | 2026-07-02 | **[SESSION 108 CONTINUATION / T-216] GG-053's "second staleness source" identified as GG-062 recurring, not a new bug — fixed via atomic `flock` singleton lock, replacing the race-prone `pkill`-based guard** | **Picked up the `run_t4_mlb_live.py` in-flight edit left untouched by the prior segment's commit (`b001441`).** GG-053's reverification (2,120 `CONTAMINATION_HALT` records, `staleness_halts=1632`, 100% staleness on the 2026-06-30→07-01 game night) had been left with an open question: GG-052's timestamp fix addressed one staleness source, but "a second, still-unidentified source" was producing the bulk of the halts. Investigated by extending GG-062's existing forensic method (duplicate/interleaved `cycle_n` IDs = proof of two concurrent processes, since `cycle_n` is monotonic-only by construction with no reset path) to the earlier 22:36–23:26 UTC segment of the same night, not just the 23:31–01:19 window GG-062 already covered. Found the identical signature — paired `C0000`/`C0001`/... cycle IDs, one series flat at `cg_halts=0` (idle orphan), the other climbing 28→716→...→1632 (real process). **Conclusion: GG-053's "second source" is GG-062, same incident, not a new bug.** GG-052's fix was correct and not implicated. **Fix implemented (T-216, previously registered but not built):** `acquire_singleton_lock()` in `run_t4_mlb_live.py` — an atomic, non-blocking `fcntl.flock(fd, LOCK_EX \| LOCK_NB)` on `logs/t4_mlb_live.lock`, acquired as the first action in `main()`, before any shared-state file (portfolio, audit log, staged-order DB) is touched. A losing process logs the conflict and `sys.exit(1)`; systemd's existing `Restart=on-failure` (60s `RestartSec`) retries until the lock frees — no crash-loop risk. Chosen over a manual PID-file because `flock` is kernel-serialized (no check-then-write race window) and self-releases on process death, crash or normal exit, so it cannot leak a stale lock across a restart. **Why this replaces, not supplements, the existing guard:** `t4-mlb.service`'s `ExecStartPre=-/usr/bin/pkill -f run_t4_mlb_live.py` (T-209) is pattern-match-and-hope — it depends on timing between the previous process's death and its deregistration, which is exactly the race GG-062 proved can leave two processes alive. Updated the service file's comment to state the pkill is now a fast-path cleanup only, not the authoritative guard. **Test coverage:** 5 new tests in `tests/test_run_t4_mlb_live.py::TestAcquireSingletonLock` (first-caller acquires, same-process reentrant call, a genuinely concurrent second holder via an independently-held `flock` on a separate fd is correctly blocked, lock is re-acquirable after the holder closes, lock file contains the holder's PID). **Verification:** full suite `pytest tests/ -q` → 2176 passed / 6 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (`test_feature_csv_freshness`, local feature CSV staleness, not from this change) — net +5 over the prior 2171/6/1 baseline, matching the 5 new tests exactly, zero regressions. mypy strict: 26 pre-existing errors in this file (GG-065 baseline), none on any line touched by this change. **Governance docs updated:** GG-062 and GG-053 both moved to RESOLVED in `GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md`, cross-referenced via `[[GG-062]]`. **Correction made before this entry was written:** GG-062's Status field, as first drafted, asserted "Deployed + `t4-mlb.service` restarted on VPS; single-process post-restart confirmed" — this had not actually happened yet at the time that text was written (drafted in anticipation of the deploy step). Corrected to state the deploy/restart/verification is the pending next step in this session's close-out, per the project's standard against asserting unverified claims in governance records. **Not yet done as of this entry:** the actual VPS deploy (`git push` → VPS `git pull` → `systemctl restart t4-mlb` → confirm single PID via `ps aux`) — next action this session. | EL |

The offline backtest runner being built this session must therefore be designed to report INCONCLUSIVE/insufficient-data gracefully rather than assume populated input. | 2026-07-02 | **[SESSION 111 / T-220] Fixed extra-innings `_compute_ab_remaining()` zero-clamp bug — T4M-PROJ/ARITH no longer go dark past inning 9** | T-220 (registered SESSION 109 CONTINUATION, LOW priority, fails-closed) tracked a known gap in `run_t4_mlb_live.py`'s `_compute_ab_remaining()`: for `inning > 9`, `innings_remaining = max(0.0, 9.0 - inning + 0.5)` clamps to 0, forcing `ab_from_inning = 0` and therefore `ab_remaining = 0` for every batter for the rest of the game once it reaches extra innings — T4M-PROJ (the live-paper-trading band this function feeds) and T4M-ARITH (which reuses the same function per its SESSION 109 CONTINUATION build) both silently stop evaluating any batter in extra innings, indefinitely, with no error or log signal. Fix: branch the `innings_remaining`/`ab_from_inning` computation on `inning <= 9` vs `inning > 9` — for extra innings, floor `ab_from_inning` at 1.0 (the batter's next likely plate appearance) rather than 0, reflecting that extra innings have no fixed endpoint the 9-inning formula's assumption depends on. `ab_from_total` (the existing ceiling, `max(0, expected_total - ab_so_far)`) is unchanged and still correctly returns 0 for a batter who has already exceeded their expected ABs for the game, even in extra innings — the fix only changes behavior for batters who have *not* yet exceeded their expected total. Updated `TestComputeAbRemaining.test_extra_innings_clamps_to_zero` (asserted the old buggy behavior) into two tests: `test_extra_innings_floors_at_one` (confirms inning 10/12 now return 1, not 0) and `test_extra_innings_still_clamps_when_already_exceeded` (confirms the ceiling still applies). Full suite: 2236 passed (net +1 from the added test) / 7 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (`test_feature_csv_freshness`) — zero regressions. This was addressed opportunistically in the same session as GG-068 (both bugs affect the same live T4-MLB matching/evaluation path) rather than deferred further, per the session's "complete all applicable T-4 components... with institutional depth" directive — T-220 was in queue, low-risk, and directly touches code this session already had open. | EL |
| 2026-07-02 | **[SESSION 111 CONTINUATION / F-FLOW-01] Built `scripts/reconstruct_mlb_score_diff.py` — retrospective, ESPN-boxscore-derived score_diff/inning reconstruction for EXP-FLOW-MLB-01, deliberately NOT wired into the live loop** | EXP-FLOW-MLB-01's own Follow-Up table names F-FLOW-01 (score_diff/inning reconstruction) as the prerequisite blocking its backtest runner — T4M-FLOW's blowout-sustain thesis needs a per-half-inning score-differential series to test against, and no such series exists anywhere in the pipeline today (T4M-FLOW's live evaluator in `t4_mlb_execution_engine.py` receives `score_diff` as a caller-supplied argument with no live source wired). **Decision:** build this retrospective/offline only, from ESPN's public FINAL-game summary endpoint (`site.api.espn.com/.../summary?event=`), which exposes per-team per-inning run totals (`linescores`) for resolved games — sufficient granularity for the design doc's inning-level "sustained gap" thesis, not sufficient for plate-appearance-level state (explicitly out of scope, noted in the module docstring). Method: for team T batting in half-inning N, `score_diff` = cumulative runs entering that half (before its own runs land) — away bats top-N off `sum(runs[:N-1])` each side, home bats bottom-N additionally crediting away's completed top-N. Output cached per `game_date` at `exp_output/EXP-FLOW-MLB-01/score_diff_reconstruction/{game_date}.json`; `load_score_diff_lookup()` reads-or-builds and returns a `(away_abbr, home_abbr, inning, half) -> record` dict for the backtest runner to join against cycle-log rows via `parse_mlb_ticker(ticker)` (new KXMLBHIT ticker parser, confirmed against real live tickers seen in production: `KXMLBHIT-26JUL021235PITPHI-PITMOZUNA24-1` etc.) + the row's own `inning`. **Explicit scope boundary carried in the module docstring:** this script does not touch `run_t4_mlb_live.py` or any live C1 path; wiring a live ESPN score_diff feed into the live 45s loop is named as a separate, later-timeline task in EXP-FLOW-MLB-01's own Follow-Up table, not undertaken here. 14 tests in `tests/test_reconstruct_mlb_score_diff.py` (ticker parsing incl. malformed-input rejection, inning-table reconstruction incl. uneven linescore-array lengths from walk-off games, ticker+inning join incl. unresolvable/out-of-range lookups) — all pure-logic, no network calls, no fixtures touching the live ESPN endpoints. | EL |

Rather than accept the INCONCLUSIVE verdict at face value, spawned a background Explore agent to determine whether `fetch_actual_hits` (`run_resolve_mlb.py`) correctly gates on game-completion status before returning a hit count. **Finding, confirmed by direct code read:** it does not — `_fetch_mlb_hits` queries the MLB Stats API `gameLog` endpoint, which updates live during a game, and returns whatever hit total is currently posted for the matching `game_date` with no check of `abstractGameState`/`codedGameState`/game status anywhere in the function. Production's `_resolve_pool()` incidentally avoids this in normal cron operation via a `game_date < today` calendar filter, but that is not a true completion gate (doubleheaders, suspended games, late-finishing games all evade it), and the new backtest runners call `fetch_actual_hits` directly with no such filter at all — exactly reproducing the failure this run exposed. **Registered GG-069** (OPEN, MEDIUM severity — correctness defect in a resolution primitive shared with production paper-trade resolution, zero capital risk today). **Correction to this run's characterization:** the EXP-ARITH-MLB-01 result must not be read as simply "insufficient data" — the 7 records that did resolve at confidence_gate=0.70 are resolved against confirmed-incomplete box scores and are known-invalid, not just small-sample. No claim of directional signal (positive or negative) should be drawn from this run under any grid cell until GG-069 is fixed and a new snapshot is frozen against independently-confirmed-FINAL games. EXP-FLOW-MLB-01 was not attempted against this same snapshot this session: F-FLOW-01's `reconstruct_mlb_score_diff.py` explicitly requires ESPN `STATUS_FINAL` before computing score_diff and returns `None` otherwise, so it would have failed closed on today's in-progress games regardless of GG-069 — correctly, since FLOW's thesis depends on real settled inning-by-inning score data that does not yet exist for today's games. | EL |

| 2026-07-02 | **[SESSION 113 CONTINUATION] Built genuine forward-time compounding Monte Carlo (`mc_forward_projection.py`) for T1/T4M-PROJ/combined, replacing the prior terminal-resolution-only MC artifact's framing; registered GG-071 (OPEN) on the kill-switch-breach finding it surfaced** | Operator rejected the prior MC artifact's approach on methodological grounds — it resolved only the open position book plus real closed P&L, not a real forward simulation ("the point of a monte carlo is to simulate and extrapolate for the purpose of projection"), and requested 1mo/6mo/1yr horizons in a genuine spaghetti/fan chart matching a supplied reference image. Calibrated from real closed trades only: T1 (n=49, 2 calendar days) and T4M-PROJ (n=12, 3 calendar days) — both explicitly disclosed as small-sample in the artifact. **Self-caught and fixed mid-build:** an initial kill-switch-probability proxy formula produced nonsensical output (100% for a band that barely moves); replaced with a `killed_by_day` boolean array tracking each path's actual permanent-kill-switch state at every simulated day, then computing `P(kill-switch hit)` as the true fraction of paths killed by that day — this produced the session's headline finding. **Finding:** T1's real, positive edge (53.06% win rate, +115.8%/−100% avg win/loss multiple) nonetheless produces a 17.05% chance of permanent 20%-drawdown kill-switch breach by day 30, rising to 43.5% by day 182 and 46.45% by day 365 — compounding a real edge at high cadence does not suppress ruin risk, it amplifies both tails simultaneously (same mechanism that also produces the six-figure median 1yr terminal bankroll). Redeployed to the existing Artifact (`mlb_mc_report.html`, https://claude.ai/code/artifact/9318e2d4-588a-4624-81c0-73b98a3a37c9) as three real Canvas-rendered spaghetti charts (60 paths each, log-scale for T1/combined, linear for T4M-PROJ) plus checkpoint tables and an adversarial caveat box; the prior terminal-resolution histogram section was preserved as a secondary reference, not deleted, per the operator's own framing ("you can use it as a reference point"). **Operator response:** "Good - wonderful findings but the compounding effect needs to be addressed in the future. | 2026-07-02 | **[T4 MLB throughput root-cause diagnosis] Earl ruled CLAUDE.md Hard Rule 9's documented T4 throughput target (~1,000 evals/game, 50-100 fires/game) is authoritative; the one-shot-per-ticker dedup implementation must change to match, not the other way around. Diagnosed by pulling real production evidence — `exp_output/MLB/snapshots/t4_mlb_cycle_log_SNAP-2026-07-02-002.jsonl` (2,393 cycle-evaluations, 2026-07-02, real GG-067 cycle-context writes, not synthetic) — cross-referenced against `run_t4_mlb_live.py`, `run_mlb_pregame.py` (PIPE-06pre), and `c4_risk_engine/risk.py`. Finding: the Bayesian/Binomial math and the C4 edge gate are both functioning correctly (a sample trajectory, `MILWCONTRERAS24-2`, shows net_edge flipping from -0.096 to +0.011 purely from a real Kalshi mid-price move, exactly the T4 latency thesis working as intended) — the volume ceiling is structural, not a signal-quality problem. Four contributing factors identified and ranked: (1) `already_fired_game` in `run_t4_mlb_live.py::_run_cycle` retires a ticker permanently after its first fire, capping the true ceiling at (distinct tickers that ever cross edge) rather than the "2-3% of ~1,000 evaluations" arithmetic CLAUDE.md's Hard Rule 9 assumes — of 63 tracked tickers on the sample day, 28 (44%) crossed net_edge>0 at least once, already close to the observed 10-20/day ceiling once C4's MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE=0.04 gate and Kelly/budget sizing are applied on top; (2) only 3 games' worth of Kalshi KXMLBHIT markets (63 tickers, 33 players) were tracked all day against what is typically a 10-15 game MLB slate — PIPE-06pre's own docstring says it pulls "ALL live KXMLBHIT markets," so this reflects Kalshi's actual thin listing coverage (consistent with CLAUDE.md's own "market efficiency gradient" thesis), not an ingestion bug, but is unconfirmed against the real day's full game count from this session (no VPS access available); (3) the captured session shows two distinct process session_ids five minutes apart and only ~69 minutes of total live coverage (19:09-20:18 UTC), well under one game's length, suggestive of a mid-window restart possibly related to the GG-072/GG-068 bug logged the same day; (4) Kalshi vig is fat-tailed (mean 13.0%, median 4.0%, max 97.0%), meaning a meaningful share of tracked markets can mathematically never clear the edge gate regardless of model quality. Earl's explicit ruling on the central tension: "At optimum means as I intended — not P/L. So each bucket fires with the correct math/filtering/decision trees/execution paths... there's a discrepancy with the dedup vs Claude - the Claude file is correct and it's something we'll need to address." This resolves the direction of the fix — CLAUDE.md's throughput language is not being revised down to match the code; the dedup/position-management policy in the code is what must be redesigned (or the addressable-market surface widened) to actually reach it. GG-076 registered documenting the full architecture-vs-documentation conflict. Five follow-up tasks registered in TODO_NEXT.md for future terminal sessions: T-225 (dedup policy redesign, HIGH, needs a DS design decision before implementation), T-226 (verify true Kalshi KXMLBHIT slate coverage vs. game count, HIGH — the single highest-leverage unconfirmed lever), T-227 (investigate the mid-window restart, MEDIUM), T-228 (evaluate a vig/liquidity floor filter, LOW), T-229 (reconcile CLAUDE.md's throughput arithmetic once T-225 lands, MEDIUM — verification only, not a downward revision). | EL |

| 2026-07-03 | **[T-225 DESIGN RULING] Earl selected "allow re-entry per ticker" as T4 MLB's replacement dedup policy, superseding the permanent one-shot-per-ticker guard.** | Presented three options per T-225's task framing (allow re-entry as new edge appears / widen tracked-market surface / a bounded cooldown mechanism). Earl's ruling and reasoning, verbatim in substance: the one-shot dedup was the right call when it was written — T4M-PROJ needed to prove the Binomial update was correct, the contamination guards actually caught stale data, the kill switch halted in time, and C4's edge gate wasn't approving garbage, before it could be trusted to fire more than once per market. A blunt permanent-retirement cap was reasonable insurance while those were unproven. But that phase is over: the kill switch has drilled at 0.34ms, the contamination guard is verified catching what it should, and production cycle-log evidence (the `MILWCONTRERAS24-2` trajectory cited in the GG-076 diagnosis) shows net_edge responding correctly to a real Kalshi price move, not noise. The mechanisms that now actually guard against over-concentration — `max_single_event_exposure_usd`, the drawdown kill switch, the T4 band budget cap — didn't exist or weren't proven when the dedup was written; keeping "never re-enter a ticker" after those exist doesn't add safety, it just under-samples. Under-sampling is a direct hit on the project's central claim (CLAUDE.md: "P&L is the score, the methodology is the asset") — the T4 thesis is that the market is slow to reprice, a claim about a repeated phenomenon, and a one-shot-per-ticker cap measures "did the bot catch the first instance of latency it happened to see," not "does the bot systematically catch market latency." That is a different, weaker experiment than the one G6/EXP-017-equivalent validation for T4 needs an answer to. Earl's explicit caveat: this ruling assumes T-226's venue-coverage question doesn't independently cap throughput below what re-entry could reach — T-226 was already RESOLVED (2026-07-02, this file, prior row) confirming real Kalshi coverage (~6-7/9 games/day) is not the primary constraint and the dedup policy is the dominant lever, so the caveat is satisfied and implementation may proceed. Closing instruction: "It made sense at the time it was done in order to maintain survival. We're proceeding now - apply accordingly." | DS |
| 2026-07-03 | **[T-225 IMPLEMENTATION] Re-entry keyed on (h_so_far, ab_remaining) state change, not a bare timer or unconditional re-fire** | Implementing "allow re-entry per ticker" required choosing what counts as a legitimate re-entry trigger. Chose to key eligibility on whether `(h_so_far, ab_remaining)` — the exact two live-state inputs to `p_hits_gte_threshold()`'s Binomial computation — has changed since the ticker's last fire, rather than a fixed cooldown timer or unconditional every-cycle re-evaluation. Rationale: this ties re-entry exactly to "has new information that could move `p_t4` actually arrived," which is the T4 latency thesis itself (market lags a real state change) rather than an arbitrary throughput knob. It also naturally bounds worst-case stacked exposure per ticker to the number of distinct `(h_so_far, ab_remaining)` states a player can pass through in one game (bounded by plate appearances, typically 4-6), not unlimited. Required adding `h_so_far_at_entry`/`ab_remaining_at_entry` fields to `PaperTrader.open_position()` (optional kwargs, default `None`, backward-compatible with T1/T2/NBA callers) so the dedup guard's per-ticker last-fired state survives a process restart via `_reconstruct_fired_tickers()` (renamed `_reconstruct_fired_ticker_states()`, now returns `dict[str, tuple[int,int]]` instead of `set[str]`). Verified no downstream code assumes ticker uniqueness across positions: `run_resolve_mlb.py::_resolve_pool()` iterates `open_positions` as a flat list filtered by `game_date`, and `PaperTrader.settle_position()` looks up positions by `position_id`, not `kalshi_ticker` — multiple concurrent positions on the same ticker resolve independently and correctly. | EL |
| 2026-07-03 | **[T-229 RESOLVED] CLAUDE.md's Hard Rule 9 / T4-alpha-thesis worked example contained a self-inconsistent arithmetic chain (480 vs. 240 cycles, 2-3% vs. real ~0.6% fire rate, ~17,000 vs. its own section-header "~1,000" figure) — corrected in place, without changing the documented 50-100-bets/game design target.** | Operator instruction, verbatim: "proceed with T-229 then address T-228" — an explicitly verification-only pass to confirm the arithmetic still matches once T-225 lands, not a downward revision.
