GG-076 Status and TODO_NEXT.md's T-229 row updated to RESOLVED to match. | EL |
| 2026-07-03 | **[T-228 RESOLVED] Added a `VIG_CEILING = 0.30` skip filter to T4M-PROJ's per-market evaluation loop (`run_t4_mlb_live.py`), placed before the Binomial p_t4 computation, not inside C4.** | Task spec (LOW, owner EL, no separate DS sign-off required) asked whether T4M-PROJ should skip/deprioritize markets above a vig ceiling given 2026-07-02's fat-tailed vig data (mean 13.0%, median 4.0%, max 97.0%). Chose the ceiling empirically rather than from the raw mean/median: queried the full 2026-07-02 `t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl` (4,971 evaluation rows) for the vig distribution of the 33 rows that actually cleared `net_edge >= 0.04` — the widest vig on any real qualifying signal was 0.24 (`KXMLBHIT-...-PHIBHARPER3-2`, p_t4=0.45, mip=0.15, net_edge=0.051). A naive reading of T-228's own suggested example (vig > 0.15) would have excluded that real signal, so 0.15 was rejected as unsafe. Set `VIG_CEILING = 0.30` instead — clear margin above the observed 0.24 max, while still cutting the fat right tail that never produced a qualifying signal in the data (477 of 4,971 rows, ~9.6%, had vig > 0.30; 966 rows, ~19.4%, had vig > 0.20). This is a pragmatic, data-grounded cutoff, not the theoretical hard ceiling (which depends on mip and is generally 0.4-0.85 given `MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE=0.04` and the 0.07x·(1-x) Kalshi fee curve) — chosen to bank the real observed savings without any evidence it clips genuine edge. **Placement decision:** the skip sits immediately after `mip`/`vig` are read from the price object and before the semantic-contract guard, `p_hits_gte_threshold()` Binomial call, and C4's `engine.evaluate_forecast()` — the three costs T-228 explicitly named avoiding. Rejected placing this inside `c4_risk_engine/risk.py` because C4 is sport-and-band-agnostic infrastructure (per CLAUDE.md architecture) and vig is a T4-MLB-specific pre-filter on which markets are worth the Binomial cost at all, not a risk-sizing decision C4 owns. **GG-067 preserved:** rather than a bare `continue` that would silently drop these rows from the cycle log (losing visibility for EXP-ARITH-MLB-01/EXP-FLOW-MLB-01 backtesting on how much volume this filter removes), the skip still writes a lightweight cycle-log record (`ts`, `ticker`, `mip`, `vig`, `vig_skip: true`, live-state fields) but omits `p_t4`/`gross_edge`/`net_edge` since the Binomial computation was never performed — the whole point of the filter. 4 new tests added: `TestRunCycle::test_no_fire_when_vig_above_ceiling`, `test_fires_at_vig_exactly_on_ceiling` (ceiling is exclusive, `vig > VIG_CEILING`), `TestProductionCycleLog::test_vig_skip_logged_without_p_t4`, `TestConstants::test_vig_ceiling_0_30`. Full suite: 2297 passed / 7 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (feature CSV staleness, same as before this change) — no regressions. | EL |
| 2026-07-03 | **[T-231] Built C8 Panel 6 — T4 MLB Detail — showing the T-4 leg in its entirety in the visual dashboard** | Earl asked, after the T-225 through T-230 confidence-gradient work, for the visual dashboard to "live wire in and show (likely with its own expanded component) the T-4 leg IN ITS ENTIRETY." Clarified scope via two questions before building (no prior instruction to skip clarification for ambiguous, production-coupled builds): (1) extend the existing C8 `dashboard.py` CLI/text architecture vs. build a new browser-based visual tool — Earl chose extending C8, matching the existing deploy path; (2) given this session has no VPS/SSH access, whether to write the code now for terminal-session deployment vs. request live access vs. prototype-only — Earl chose write-now-deploy-later, the same handoff pattern already established for T-225 through T-230. | 2026-07-03 | **[Item 1: GG-004/EXP-017 MLB commercial validation] Ran T-184's existing `scripts/run_exp017_mlb.py` against real MLB portfolio data for the first time (n=49); avoided duplicating existing work; found and fixed two bugs in the loader (`kalshi_threshold` field mismatch, `fee_net_edge` sign convention); flagged a deeper, unfixed production-side finding as GG-077.** | Standing operator instruction: "complete the first two [priority items] with full institutional depth, then report back prior to starting 3-5." Item 1 was to run EXP-017 against real MLB data to answer GG-004. Before writing new analysis code, checked `tests/` naming conventions and discovered T-184 (SESSION 79, 2026-06-23) had already built exactly this tool (`scripts/run_exp017_mlb.py` + `tests/test_t184_exp017_mlb.py`, 99 tests) — written when the MLB portfolio was empty ("INCONCLUSIVE as expected") and dormant since. A duplicate script had already been written and run successfully by this session before the discovery; deleted it immediately (`rm src/prediction_market_bot/run_exp017_mlb.py`) and switched to the real tool, consistent with this project's verify-before-build culture. Downloaded the real 49-position `paper_portfolio_mlb.json` from the VPS and ran `scripts/run_exp017_mlb.py` against it. **Bug 1 (fixed):** the loader's `_load_portfolio()` read `p.get("threshold")`, but the real producer (`run_mlb_paper_trade.py`/PIPE-05, via `paper_trader.py`) writes `kalshi_threshold` — every real position was silently bucketing to threshold k=0. Fixed with a fallback (`p.get("kalshi_threshold", p.get("threshold", 0))`); 2 regression tests added; full suite re-verified passing; re-run confirmed correct k=1/k=2 buckets. **Bug 2 (fixed, analysis-side only):** noticed the Edge Monotonicity check's `edge>0 n=2 / edge<=0 n=47` split exactly matched the real portfolio's YES/NO direction split (2 YES, 47 NO) — not a coincidence. Traced through `paper_trader.py::open_position()` (lines 380-460) and confirmed the stored `fee_net_edge` field is computed `gross_edge - fee_per_contract` where `gross_edge` is always YES-frame-signed and never flipped for direction — even though the same function DOES flip sign for a sibling field, `fee_contaminated`, three lines later (`_directional_gross = gross_edge if direction == "YES" else -gross_edge`), proving the omission wasn't intentional. Fixed the loader to stop trusting any stored `fee_net_edge` value under any circumstance, always recomputing via the already-correct, already-tested `compute_fee_net_edge(direction, model_p, mip)`; replaced the now-contradicted test `test_uses_stored_fee_net_edge_when_present` with two tests asserting the loader ignores a wrong-signed stored value and recomputes correctly for both directions. Full suite: 102/102 passing. Re-ran the script: Edge Monotonicity now reads `edge>0 n=49 / edge<=0 n=0` (all real fired bets do carry genuinely positive direction-effective edge — the direction-split artifact is gone) with all three edge terciles positive; Monotonicity verdict INCONCLUSIVE (expected — the bot only fires above its edge threshold, so there's no natural negative-edge comparison group in resolved data). **Real result, unaffected by either bug (both bugs were loader/analysis-only, not P&L-affecting):** n=49 unique markets, EV=+$2.0408/bet, win rate 53.1%, total P&L +$100.00, verdict **DIRECTIONAL_POSITIVE** — below both ADR-0024 Tier 1 (n≥150) and Tier 2 (n≥500 single/n≥150 dual-sport) floors, so GG-004 remains OPEN pending further accumulation. **Deeper finding, NOT fixed this session (registered as GG-077, flagged for operator/DS review):** the same YES-frame-always pattern likely affects `run_mlb_paper_trade.py`'s own production `edge` value — the one that feeds C4 `risk.py` CHECK 3's `abs(edge) >= MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE` gate. Since `abs()` of a YES-frame quantity is not equal to the true direction-effective edge once vig/half-spread and the Kalshi fee are both accounted for (derived: true NO edge = `-gross_edge - fee`, vs. the system's `abs(gross_edge - fee)`), and 47 of 49 real positions (96%) are NO-direction, this is a small but systematic mis-gating that has been present in every live NO bet since PIPE-05 activation. This touches live, cron-active betting logic and was deliberately NOT modified this session — surfaced as GG-077 for explicit operator/DS review rather than silently patched, per this project's capability-boundary language (verify and surface, do not replace the operator's architectural judgment on core betting logic). GG-004 and GG-077 both updated in `GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md` with full detail. | EL |

| 2026-07-04 | **[GG-081/T-236 + GG-083/T-238 RESOLVED/CLOSED] Third and fourth items of the sequenced work: investigated and fixed the TBJCAMINERO13-1/-2 double-fire (same `(h_so_far, ab_remaining)` state, ~18 min apart) surfaced during the GG-080 validation pass.** Ground-truth verification against `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` and `t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl` (not log inference alone) confirmed both fires shared the same `session_id` (`T4-MLB-F4F94E58`) — ruling out a process restart and confirming a live, same-process logic defect, not stale pre-T-225 data. **Root cause:** `run_t4_mlb_live.py`'s UTC-midnight date-rollover block reassigns `game_date` to the new calendar day, then `.clear()`s and reconstructs `already_fired_game` (the dedup guard) filtered on that new day's `game_date` string alone. A game that started the evening before local time (first pitch 2026-07-02 19:40 local ≈ 23:40 UTC) has its already-open positions persisted with the *prior* day's `game_date` string — so the instant the clock crosses midnight, the reconstruction finds zero matching positions for that still-live game and the dedup guard silently loses all memory of its prior fire, for the rest of that game. Confirmed this predates T-225 (present in the old `_reconstruct_fired_tickers` too) and is orthogonal to T-225's one-shot-vs-state-tuple redesign — T-225 gets neither credit nor blame. **While designing the fix, found an unrelated sibling defect (registered fresh as GG-083/T-238 in the same pass):** `_reconstruct_t4_ledger` — which computes `t4_deployed_game`, the value the T4 band-budget cap is checked against — has the identical single-`game_date`-string filter bug at the identical rollover call site, silently understating open exposure for any carried-over game after midnight. Not confirmed to have caused an actual limit breach (both `max_single_event_exposure_usd` and the drawdown kill switch are independent backstops), but a capital-control-input correctness defect in principle more consequential than GG-081's dedup nuisance. **Fix (single commit, both defects):** new helper `_game_date_window(game_date: str) -> {game_date, prior_day}`; `_reconstruct_t4_ledger` and `_reconstruct_fired_ticker_states` both widened from `game_date: str` to `game_dates: Union[str, set]` (backward-compatible via an `isinstance` check — a bare string still filters to exactly that one day, so all pre-existing unit tests needed zero changes), with the equality filter changed from `==` to `in`. All 4 call sites (2 at cold start, 2 at UTC rollover) updated to pass `_game_date_window(game_date)` instead of the bare string. Safety argument: Kalshi tickers embed the actual game date and matchup (e.g. `KXMLBHIT-26JUL021940TBKC-...`), so widening the reconstruction window by one day carries no cross-game collision risk — it can only ever add positions from a genuinely distinct game/date pairing, never conflate two games. 6 new regression tests added (`TestGameDateWindow`, `TestReconstructT4LedgerCrossMidnight`, `TestReconstructFiredTickerStatesCrossMidnight`) in `tests/test_run_t4_mlb_live.py`. Full suite: 2339 passed / 7 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (feature-CSV staleness, unchanged, untouched) — zero regressions. Deployed via `git push` + VPS `git pull` + `systemctl restart t4-mlb` (this fix touches the live systemd-managed 24/7 polling service, not a per-invocation cron script, so a restart — not just a pull — was required for the fix to take effect). GG-081 and GG-083 both closed to RESOLVED; T-236 and T-238 both closed. | 2026-07-05 | **[GG-084/T-239 RESOLVED — MLB Stats API 406 root-caused as an IP-based Fastly-CDN block, not request cadence; circuit breaker + degraded-resolution alert landed in `run_resolve_mlb.py`]** The prior registration of this gap (2026-07-04 row above) left the root cause as an open hypothesis ("possibly bursty/cadence-related on our side") and no fix implemented. This session characterized it directly: identical `gameLog` requests issued from the VPS datacenter IP (DigitalOcean 157.230.91.33) and a residential IP, retested ~2 hours apart against the same player/date combo that had resolved successfully from the datacenter IP hours earlier, showed the datacenter IP returning HTTP 406 on both `gameType=R` and `gameType=P` while the residential IP returned HTTP 200 throughout — falsifying the cadence/request-parameter hypothesis and the module's prior docstring claim that the current season is "reliably accessible from datacenter IP." Confirmed this is actively degrading live production resolution, not just ad hoc EXP backfill runs: today's 06:30 UTC `resolve_mlb_cron.log` run showed 68/83 eligible positions (82%) skipped across the T1/T2 and T4 pools. Implemented in `run_resolve_mlb.py::_fetch_mlb_hits`: a circuit breaker that, after `_CIRCUIT_BREAKER_THRESHOLD=5` consecutive full-retry exhaustions in a process, collapses the retry/backoff schedule to a single cheap probe attempt per call rather than skipping the call outright — an initial fully-skip design was built first and rejected after its own regression test caught the flaw: skipping the call entirely means no call can ever reach the reset line, so the breaker could never observe recovery once open. The corrected design still probes every call (cheaply) and resets `_consecutive_api_failures` to 0 the moment a response gets past `raise_for_status()`. Also added a new `degraded_resolution_rate` alert in `_resolve_pool()` (fires when resolved/eligible < 50% on batches of n≥5) to close a real monitoring gap: GG-048's existing zero-resolved alert only fires at 100% failure and produced no signal on today's 82%-failure run. No data-loss exposure — confirmed via code inspection that `mark_aged_open()` never removes a position from `open_positions`, so unresolved positions retry on every subsequent daily cron run indefinitely; the cost of this gap is delayed P&L settlement/visibility, not lost bets. Module docstring corrected to remove the falsified accessibility claim. 5 new regression tests added to `tests/test_t187e_mlb_resolve.py` (2 circuit-breaker, 3 degraded-alert); full suite green at 2,349 passed (1 pre-existing, unrelated failure: `test_feature_csv_freshness`, stale feature CSV — not touched by this fix). | 2026-07-05 | **[GG-086/T-240 RESOLVED — `_reconstruct_t4_ledger`'s open-exposure sum was still date-filtered, permanently dropping positions stuck open past 24 hours; fixed to never date-filter open exposure]** Found while sequentially verifying GG-081/GG-083's UTC-midnight-rollover fix against the real 2026-07-05 00:00 UTC rollover (11 carried-over games, exactly the scenario that fix targets): the T4 band-budget log showed `budget_used` DROP from $20.86 (23:59:59, pre-rollover) to $15.86 (00:01:53, post-rollover) instead of being preserved across a carryover event. Direct inspection of the live `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` on the VPS found the cause: 22 pool_isolated open positions dated 2026-07-02 ($7.69) and 11 dated 2026-07-03 ($5.09) — $12.78 combined, matching the observed drop — were still open 2-3 days past their `game_date`, almost certainly stuck by GG-084's MLB Stats API IP block preventing `run_resolve_mlb.py` from resolving them. `_game_date_window()` only ever widens to `{game_date, prior_day}` (one day back), so once a *second* midnight passes without a position resolving, it falls out of every future reconstruction permanently — not just at the boundary it crossed. This let the risk engine believe there was more budget headroom than truly existed, allowing new fires to stack on top of invisible real exposure past the intended $270/day T4 band cap. Fix: `_reconstruct_t4_ledger()`'s `open_exposure` term no longer filters by `game_date` — it sums `size_usd` across ALL currently-open `pool_isolated` positions regardless of age, since an open position is real deployed capital until it resolves, no matter how many days that takes. `realized_losses` (closed positions) keeps the date-window filter — attributing a realized loss to a specific day's budget is a legitimately dated concept and was not part of the bug. Two existing tests (`test_excludes_open_different_date`, `test_two_days_prior_still_excluded`) had encoded the bug as expected behavior and were rewritten to assert the corrected behavior; 3 new tests added, including one reproducing the real production numbers directly ($7.69 + $5.09 = $12.78, both cohorts must count). Confirmed the `already_fired_game` ticker-dedup half of GG-081/GG-083 does NOT share this exposure — a stale ticker for a game that ended days ago can never reappear in a live ESPN poll (`game_ids` only tracks currently-live games), so it's inert, not a live risk. Full regression suite green (2,310 passed / 1 skipped / 0 new failures). A second, previously undocumented bug surfaced and fixed in the same change: the flat T4 paper-sizing policy `approved_size = mip` ("1 contract at market price") is YES-price-only; a NO contract's market price is `1 - mip`, so this became `approved_size = mip if direction == "YES" else (1.0 - mip)`. The FIRED log line and module docstring's "Fire condition" section were both updated to reflect the bidirectional condition (docstring previously described YES-only). A structurally identical pattern was then found in `PLAN.md`'s own "Last reviewed" line (25,357 characters) and trimmed the same way. No file/format change was made to establish a going-forward policy (e.g., no header comment added instructing future sessions to trim at N characters) — this entry is the record of the judgment call itself, to be consulted if the same "Last reviewed" bloat recurs. | A single-line field with no per-entry structure that silently breaks the Read tool is a different failure mode than a long-but-structured section, and warrants a different fix (trim to current-state) rather than the same accretive convention (prepend new, keep all old) used elsewhere in these files — conflating the two would either over-trim readable history or leave the broken-Read problem unresolved. | EL |

| 2026-07-07 | **[SESSION 124 — GG-095 RESOLVED, T-247: `monitor.py`'s L3/L4 Brier ladder bug fixed — kill switch no longer auto-fires from drift alone]** Operator instruction: "Proceed with L4 design - this was the intended design that had not been built out initially." Full read of `monitor.py`'s own module docstring (lines 27-39, unchanged since authorship) resolved the (a)/(b) ambiguity left open by the prior entry: the docstring already documented a 3-tier Brier ladder (`drift_alert_l1_watch`/`l2_alert`/`l3_pause`, the last at ERROR severity/ALT-002) with no Brier-driven L4 event ever listed — meaning `monitor.py`'s relative-delta/3-level design (not T-094's 2026-06-02 absolute/4-level entry) was always the intended design; the code just never correctly implemented it. Confirmed via direct grep of `risk.py` (lines 216, 219, 563) that the real L4/kill switch already exists and works correctly, driven independently by `MAX_DAILY_LOSS_PCT`/`max_drawdown_pct` — exactly matching `MONITORING_DRIFT_PLAN.md`'s ladder table ("L4 — Any kill threshold breach, see RISK_APPETITE_CONSTRAINTS.md"). The actual shipped bug was narrow: `get_drift_snapshot()` assigned `alert_level = ALERT_L4_KILL` (not `ALERT_L3_PAUSE`) the moment Brier crossed `BRIER_L3_DELTA` — a straight wrong-constant assignment that left `ALERT_L3_PAUSE` fully wired in `check_and_alert()`'s logging maps (correct ERROR severity, correct `drift_alert_l3_pause` event name) but structurally unreachable, so every L3-level Brier breach was silently auto-firing a full, undocumented kill switch instead of a pause-tier alert. Fix: `get_drift_snapshot()` now sets `ALERT_L3_PAUSE` at the `BRIER_L3_DELTA` crossing; `check_and_alert()`'s L4 auto-kill branch is retained (dormant, for a possible future integration reflecting risk.py's independent kill state into a unified `DriftSnapshot`) but can no longer be reached from this module's own Brier computation. Updated 4 tests in `tests/test_c7_monitor.py` to match (renamed/inverted where the old test encoded the bug's behavior; added one exercising the now-dormant L4 branch directly via a patched snapshot so its idempotency logic stays covered). Full suite: 2,374 passed / 7 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (feature CSV staleness). **Explicitly not built this session:** `MONITORING_DRIFT_PLAN.md`'s ladder table describes L3 as auto-halting new position opens specifically, not merely logging a differently-labeled event — no such enforcement (e.g., RiskEngine consulting C7's alert_level before approving a forecast) exists anywhere in the codebase (confirmed via grep, no `pause`/`halt_new`/`block_new` hits in `risk.py`/`paper_trader.py`). Building that is a distinct C4↔C7 architectural wire beyond this fix's scope and was surfaced to the operator as an open follow-up rather than built unilaterally. T-094 (2026-06-02) is superseded by this entry, not edited (immutable log). | The prior entry's own investigation had already ruled out guessing at the right answer; the missing piece was simply reading `monitor.py`'s own docstring closely enough to see it had documented the correct 3-tier design from the start — the bug was an implementation slip (wrong constant assigned), not an unresolved design ambiguity between two competing intents. Retaining the dormant L4 branch rather than deleting it preserves a plumbing path for a future cross-component integration without adding untested surface area now. Not building new C4↔C7 enforcement wiring in the same pass keeps this fix scoped to what was asked ("L4 design" = correct the ladder, confirm L4 already lives correctly in risk.py) rather than silently expanding into a new architectural decision the operator hasn't reviewed. | GG-095, T-247 | EL |

Documented and locked in as its own dedicated test (`test_exact_decile_inputs_can_legitimately_diverge_between_implementations`) rather than silently avoided by choosing non-boundary test data — the boundary-agreement test itself was kept, using near-but-not-exact-decile inputs. | 2026-07-09 | **[B3 (T-280) — Hypothesis property-based invariant tests added; found a real float64 sigmoid-saturation edge case in temperature_scale under sharpening temperatures]** Added `hypothesis` as a proper dev dependency (`pyproject.toml` dev extras `hypothesis>=6.100.0,<7.0`; pinned `hypothesis==6.156.4` in `requirements-dev.txt`, installed against the interpreter pytest actually resolves to — `/usr/local/opt/python@3.11/bin/python3.11`, not `/usr/bin/python3` 3.9.6). Built `tests/test_t280_property_based_invariants.py` (14 tests, fixed-example-sequence via `settings.register_profile("t280-deterministic", derandomize=True, max_examples=100)` rather than a manual per-test seed) covering: Kelly sizing (bounded by full-Kelly bankroll fraction, never exceeds `_HARD_LIMITS["max_single_event_exposure_usd"]`, monotonic non-decreasing in `p_estimate`); temperature scaling (monotonic ranking preserved, small-positive-T never raises, zero/negative-T raises); Brier score (bounded [0,1] on arbitrary generated datasets, zero when perfect, one when maximally wrong); binomial PMF (`run_t4_mlb_live.py::_binomial_pmf`, loaded via `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` to bypass its BOT_ENV/BOT_C4_ACTIVE guard — log-space agrees with a naive `math.comb` reference across a generated `n∈[1,15]`/`p∈[0.001,0.999]`/`k≤n` domain, sums to ~1 over full support, always bounded [0,1]). Property-based generation surfaced a genuine finding the fixed-example B2 suite could not have found: the general "T≥1.0 keeps output strictly inside the open interval (0,1)" property (correctly scoped to production's real T-range, since `CALIBRATION_TEMPERATURE` only ever defines T≥1.0) does NOT extend to T<1.0 (sharpening) — Hypothesis found `temperature_scale(p=1.0, temperature=0.5)` returns exactly `1.0` in float64, not the mathematically-open value the closed form implies. Root-caused directly (not just observed): `sigmoid(x)=1/(1+exp(-x))` saturates to exactly `1.0` once `exp(-x)` underflows below float64's relative epsilon (~2.22e-16) such that `1.0+exp(-x)` rounds to exactly `1.0`; confirmed the precise threshold by direct probe — `temperature_scale(1.0, T)` returns exactly `1.0` for `T≤~0.55`, while `T=0.6` is the largest sub-1.0 temperature still observed unsaturated (`0.9999999999999989`). Confirmed asymmetric: the `p→0` side does not saturate to exactly `0.0` under the same conditions (`temperature_scale(0.0, 0.5)==1.0000000020000015e-18`), because underflow-to-zero (~1e-308) is a far more extreme float threshold than the "1+ε==1" addition-rounding threshold that bites the `p→1` side. Locked this in as its own dedicated test (`test_sharpening_temperature_can_saturate_extreme_p_to_exact_boundary`) with the exact reproduction values, rather than silently narrowing the Hypothesis strategy to dodge the failure — same "document the finding, don't hide it" discipline B2 established for the ECE bin-edge divergence. Severity assessed as negligible/unreached in production: both `CALIBRATION_TEMPERATURE` entries (`nba: 2.05`, `mlb: 1.0`) are ≥1.0, so the sharpening branch where this saturates is never exercised by live code. Two self-identified authoring errors were caught and fixed before commit, not left in: an awkward compound assertion in the first draft of the open-interval test (fixed via `assume(temperature != 1.0)` instead of an `or`-branch); and a transcription error where the dedicated finding test's "still-unsaturated" example initially used `T=0.55` (which is actually already saturated) instead of the correct `T=0.6` — caught by re-running direct verification probes before trusting the drafted values, not by a failing assertion alone. Full suite after this addition: 2518 passed, 30 skipped, 0 failed, 1 pre-existing unrelated warning (same as B2's baseline plus 14 new tests). Committed `05142f9` (test file, `pyproject.toml`, `requirements-dev.txt`), pushed to `origin session/20260709T000817Z-window-b-math-rigor`. | Property-based generation is specifically valuable for finding exactly this class of boundary bug — a fixed-example suite (B2) would need to guess the precise saturating input by hand, while Hypothesis's shrinking found and minimized it automatically. Rescoping the general property to T≥1.0 (production's real range) rather than either weakening it to hide the finding or deleting it kept both the correctly-scoped invariant and the genuine finding visible, matching the same choice B2 made for its ECE divergence. Treating this as unreached-in-production rather than an emergency fix is consistent with the severity standard already applied to B2's finding: real, precisely documented, but not currently capital-relevant given the live `CALIBRATION_TEMPERATURE` registry never uses T<1.0. | T-280 | Claude (Window B, isolated worktree `session/20260709T000817Z-window-b-math-rigor`) |

| 2026-07-10 | **[T-282 DONE — GG-109 RESOLVED: systemic import-resolution fix via `pythonpath = ["src"]`]** Audited all 70 files under `tests/` for the missing-`sys.path.insert(worktree_src)`-guard pattern GG-109 identified from a single file in B4: **25 files already carried the guard, 31 did not** — confirming the gap was systemic, not a one-off. Rather than patching 31 files individually, added `pythonpath = ["src"]` to `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` in `pyproject.toml` — pytest's native, core (no plugin) mechanism that prepends a rootdir-relative path to `sys.path` before test collection begins; since `pythonpath` resolves relative to the config file's own directory, each worktree's own `pyproject.toml` makes that worktree's own `src/` win the import race unconditionally, for every file, guarded or not. Verified the fix is real rather than assumed: wrote a throwaway probe test (`tests/test_probe_gg104.py`, deleted after use) with a bare, unguarded `import prediction_market_bot` asserting `risk._MIP_EPSILON` — a confirmed worktree-only symbol from B1, absent from the main tree — is present; it passed and printed the resolved module path as the worktree's own `src/`, not main's. Re-ran the full suite post-fix as the actual gate: **2,541 passed / 30 skipped / 0 failed** — identical to the pre-fix count, confirming the fix closes a real risk without changing any test's behavior (i.e. no prior full-suite run had actually been silently validating main-tree code — the fragility GG-109 flagged was real but had not yet produced a false positive). Updated `GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md` GG-109 (Resolution/Status → RESOLVED) and `TODO_NEXT.md`'s T-282 row in place. Left the 25 existing per-file guards untouched (redundant but harmless under the new ini setting, not worth the churn of removing them). B5's own requirement for a global seeded `tests/conftest.py` (determinism, not import resolution) remains a separate, unstarted item — the `pythonpath` ini fix does not need a `conftest.py`, so B5 will create one fresh for its own purpose rather than retrofit this fix into it. | Auditing all 70 files rather than trusting the one-file B4 finding as representative was the correct call — 31 unguarded files is a materially different risk profile than 1, and would have justified the systemic fix on its own even without the B4 discovery. Choosing pytest's native `pythonpath` ini option over a custom `conftest.py` sys.path hack keeps the fix to one line in an already-existing config section, applies uniformly regardless of collection order or which file imports first, and needs no per-file changes — the simpler mechanism that fully closes the gap beats a more elaborate one that does the same job. Verifying against a real, confirmed cross-tree divergence (`_MIP_EPSILON`) rather than a synthetic marker file proves the fix defeats the actual failure mode GG-109 was registered for, not just a proxy for it. | GG-109, T-282 | Claude (Window B, isolated worktree `session/20260709T000817Z-window-b-math-rigor`) |

Chose full-suite-on-every-push over a narrower "just these two files" job because a partial CI gate that certifies band-monitor/power-calc green while silently permitting regressions elsewhere in the other ~2,400 tests would be a false sense of security — worse than no gate, since it would look green. Pinned-lockfile install (over floating `pyproject.toml` ranges) matches this repo's own reproducibility convention (`DATA_SNAPSHOTS_INDEX.md`/Data-Snapshot Discipline extends the same reproducibility principle to dependency versions) and ensures CI can't silently diverge from the certified-green version set. | Claude (T-337/N-02, Window 1) |
